### `fdda5a896` — fix(hangar): complete C6 - drop training wrappers orphaned by the card deletion

_Claude, 2026-07-25 02:39:37 +0000_

```text
C6 deleted the retired solo arcade cards but left the SO_TrainingGame_*
wrappers that pointed at them, so four wrappers deserialized with a null
Game. The path is live, not theoretical: two Menu_Main buttons call
HangarScreen.DisplayTrainingModal, whose "TrainingGames != null" guard
passes on a non-null list, and HangarTrainingModal.SelectGame then
dereferences SelectedGame.Game.DisplayName - an NRE on the Train button
for Manta, Dolphin, Serpent and Squirrel. Verified against the merge base:
all four wrapper targets existed there, so this is C6 fallout, not merge
fallout.

- Delete SO_TrainingGame_{BlockBandit,Darts,MazeRunner,SlipAndStride}: their
  entire content was a pointer to a card C6 removed.
- Prune them from SO_Class_{Manta,Dolphin,Serpent,Squirrel} (each listed its
  wrapper twice, so the modal's two buttons showed the same game anyway).
- Delete TrainingGames.asset: every element was one of the four, leaving an
  empty list with zero referrers - the same "empty list + its UI surface"
  cleanup applied in C11.

Also fixes the entry-point guard, which was wrong independently of the above:
SetTrainingGames indexes [0] and [1] unconditionally, so a null check never
protected it. Grizzly, Termite and Urchin already had empty lists at the merge
base and would have thrown ArgumentOutOfRangeException there too. The guard
now requires two games.

The surface stays wired rather than retired - bleeding-edge added
SO_TrainingGame_Rampage pointing at a live card, which reads as intent to
repopulate training with the surviving multiplayer modes. That wrapper is
left untouched; it is currently in no list.
```

### `0243abb3f` — refactor(rampage): fold the end-game tail onto the shared SyncFinalResults template

_Claude, 2026-07-25 02:41:57 +0000_

```text
Rampage arrived from bleeding-edge with a hand-copied end-game tail: its own
_finalResultsSent latch, a SyncFinalScoresSnapshot/SyncFinalScores_ClientRpc
pair, and a SetupNewRound guard. That was correct when it was written - it
predates the Y1.2 hoist - but after the merge it left one of six domain modes
off the template, which is exactly the duplication Y1.2 removed.

The fold is exact, not approximate:
- The rule's metric is PrismsDestroyed, so the base's rule.LiveMetric snapshot
  and ScoringMetrics.Write replicate the same HostilePrismsDestroyed field the
  bespoke arrays carried.
- ResolveWinnerRepresentativeName breaks ties on the first roster entry, which
  is what the bespoke OrderByDescending(HostilePrismsDestroyed) already did.
- The base owns the SetupNewRound suppression (FinalResultsSent) and the latch
  reset in OnResetForReplayCustom, so both overrides were pure duplicates.
- gameData.ScoringRule = rule dropped: the base already publishes it, null
  guarded, so the unconditional re-assignment only defeated that guard.

Fixes two latent defects the copy carried:
- gameData.HasNoWinner was never written by Rampage. Masked today only because
  ResetRuntimeData clears it on every scene load, so a stale true could not
  survive - latent, but a DNF would not have rendered correctly.
- Winner* was written BEFORE SetResults, the inverse of the documented order.
  SetResults derives Winner* from Results[0] when unset, which is why the
  template writes them after.

No serialized state changed: the scene's seven keys on the component
(ShowTopMostFoldoutHeaderGroup, numberOfRounds, numberOfTurnsPerRound,
countdownTimer, _onToggleReadyButton, rule, arenaCell, aiRetargetSeconds) all
still map to live fields, and rule still resolves to RampageScoringRule.asset.
Removing a ClientRpc changes only the RPC table, which is same-build safe.

All six domain modes now share the one tail. Verify with test step 29.
```

 Docs/UnifiedSystems/YASH.md                            |  13 ++++++
 2 files changed, 25 insertions(+), 105 deletions(-)

```text
Both are prefab-instance m_Modifications entries whose property no longer
exists on the target script, so Unity silently discards them on load and
re-serializes - meaning anyone who opened either scene got a dirty file they
did not edit. They were also the last references anywhere to two deleted
assets.

- MinigameRampage.unity: "domainColorPalette" override on the Scoreboard
  component, pointing at DomainColorPalette.asset. Both the asset and
  DomainColorPaletteSO were deleted on bleeding-edge; no live script declares
  the field.

  FeedEntry.prefab. Doubly stale - the GameEventFeed system was deleted with
  the toast migration, and the override's target fileID no longer exists in
  GameCanvas-HexRace.prefab either.

Neither existed on my branch alone; both arrived with bleeding-edge and were
inert there too, so this is cleanup rather than a merge fix. After removal
both scenes lint clean (81 and 89 documents, all anchors unique, every
remaining override a well-formed target/propertyPath/value/objectReference
quad) and both deleted GUIDs now have zero referrers repo-wide.

### `2c7f0656c` — fix(arcade): CS0103 - client config RPC body used a parameter C5 removed

_Claude, 2026-07-30 19:21:37 +0000_

```text
SyncGameConfigToClients_ClientRpc lost its isMultiplayer parameter when C5
retired IsMultiplayerMode (every mode runs the networked single-host model, so
there is nothing left to sync). Their Load Time Insights work added a
LoadInsights.SetGameContext call inside that same method body which passes the
parameter. Each side only touched its own half, so the merge produced my
signature with their body and no conflict marker:

  Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Arcade/MultiplayerMiniGameControllerBase.cs(533,79):
  error CS0103: The name 'isMultiplayer' does not exist in the current context

Passes isMultiplayer: true, matching the identical fix already applied to
GameDataSO.InvokeGameLaunch in the merge commit. The solo-vs-party distinction
the flag used to carry is readable off humanPlayers, which the same call
already reports.

Why the merge sweep missed it: that sweep grepped for the deleted TYPE and
MEMBER names (IsMultiplayerMode, SO_Game.IsMultiplayer). This was a lowercase
local parameter, invisible to those greps. Added a check for the actual failure
shape - a merged method signature that dropped a parameter its merged body still
uses - validated by confirming it fires on this exact bug, then run across all
88 both-modified files that survive in the tree: no other instance. A companion
call-site arity check over the four methods whose parameter count differs
between the two parents (SyncGameConfigToClients_ClientRpc, AssignLineage,
SpawnFaunaPopulation, ShowAsync) is also clean - declaration and call site agree
in every case.
```

### `6d0e89087` — docs(presence): plan push-based presence sync + PresenceStateMachine

_Claude, 2026-08-01 20:07:03 +0000_

```text
Audits the presence/party/friends layer against the three reported
symptoms (stale or wrong FriendsListPanel online rows, stale
ArcadeLobbyList party slots, wrong profile icons) and lands a sequenced
plan. Every root cause carries a file:line and was adversarially
re-verified against source.

Root causes (abridged):

- No push path exists. The presence lobby is already an ISession, and
  the repo already consumes ISession.PlayerLeaving/Deleted on the PARTY
  session, but no ISession event is subscribed on the presence lobby.
  Discovery is 100% poll.
- Two catch branches in HostConnectionService.RefreshAsync are literal
  no-ops, and the lobby read is the first await, so an SDK stale-index
  throw voids the whole tick (online diff, invite scan, member sync,
  publish) indistinguishably from a healthy poll.
- IsRateLimitException does not walk InnerException while its two
  siblings do, so a wrapped 429 escalates the ForceReset watchdog.
- AppManager hardcodes new LobbyRefreshScheduler(1.5f); the
  refreshIntervalSeconds inspector field never reaches the scheduler.
- No OnApplicationQuit/Pause handler exists; the only leave is an
  async void OnDestroy that awaits after teardown began.
- Departure has no grace: one short read evicts the whole roster.
- matchName is structurally unpublishable (ResolveCurrentMatchName
  returns empty while on a menu scene, HandleGameLaunch fires while
  Menu_Main is still active, and the loop is dead in game scenes), so
  OnlineInfoEntry.Status.InMatch is dead code.
- A presence-lobby reconnect Clear()s a live party roster.
- ArcadeLobbyList.OnEnable fires once per scene, not per open: arcade
  modals hide by CanvasGroup and never SetActive(false).
- Avatar id 0 is the universal sentinel but the icon list starts at
  Id 1, and all seven duplicated resolvers fall back to element [0] —
  so "unknown" renders as a real, plausible icon.
- Player.NetAvatarId has no observable; configure-modal chips sample it
  once and subscribe only to NetDomain.

Design: a PresenceStateMachine SIBLING to PartyStateMachine (orthogonal
axis - you are InParty AND InMatch), with Offline/Joining/Announced/
Present/InMatch/Recovering/Departing, driven off GameDataSO.OnClientReady
for the lava-lamp vessel spawn and GameDataSO.OnLaunchGame for match
entry. Remote ingestion via ISession push callbacks that set an int flag
only (no Unity API, no SOAP raise) drained on the main thread in Update,
which is both the thread guarantee and a per-frame coalescer. Rate-limit
budget goes from over-cap to ~0.1 reads/s steady state; property writes
drop from 3 UGS calls to 1.

Also documents honestly what UGS can and cannot guarantee for removal
(sub-second graceful, ~10s party peer via transport, service-side reap
for non-party hard kills) and replaces instant eviction with three-value
liveness tombstones.

Docs: dedupes the party/presence/roadmap backlogs into one prioritized
list, records six doc/code drifts found (incl. the stale lazy-Relay
comment at HostConnectionService.cs:481-485), closes PRESENCE-TODO-P7 as
won't-do with rationale, sequences the PresenceLobbyService refactor, and
records five claims examined but NOT carried forward so they are not
re-derived.

No code changed.
```

 Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md | 733 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
 Docs/PresenceSystem/REFACTOR.md           |   5 +
 Docs/PresenceSystem/TODOS.md              |  31 ++-

 5 files changed, 769 insertions(+), 8 deletions(-)

The presence lobby is already an `ISession`; the repo already consumes `ISession.PlayerLeaving`/`Deleted` on the *party* session — it was simply never subscribed on the presence lobby.
 
 ### Med

diff --git a/Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md b/Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..d4bf85615
--- /dev/null
+++ b/Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md
@@ -0,0 +1,733 @@
+# Presence Sync — Plan
+
+**Goal (as stated).** When any player anywhere enters the world (their Menu_Main lava-lamp
+vessel spawns), that fact is broadcast to every other client and their `FriendsListPanel`
+repaints. When a player leaves, they disappear from every other client's `FriendsListPanel`
+/ `ArcadeLobbyList` as fast as the platform allows. Both driven by an explicit state machine
+so each step is an observable event other systems can subscribe to.
+
+**Status.** Plan only — no code changed yet. Every claim below carries a `file:line` and was
+adversarially re-verified against source. Corrections from the verification pass are folded in;
+where a claim did not survive it is listed in § 8 rather than quietly dropped.
+
+**Scope.** The three reported symptoms:
+
+| | Symptom |
+|---|---|
+| **A** | `FriendsListPanel` online rows are wrong or late |
+| **B** | `ArcadeLobbyList` party slots are wrong or late |
+| **C** | Profile icons in the arcade screen / configure modal are wrong |
+
+---
+
+## 1. Why it's wrong today, in one paragraph
+
+There is **no push path anywhere in the presence layer**. `HostConnectionService.Update()`
+(`:377-388`) ticks a timer and fire-and-forgets `RefreshAsync()`, which `GET`s the lobby and
+diffs the roster. The presence lobby is already an `ISession` — the same type whose
+`PlayerLeaving` and `Deleted` events this repo *already consumes on the party session*
+(`PartySessionService.cs:186,240`) — but **no `ISession` event is subscribed on the presence
+lobby at all**. So "a player arrived" and "a player left" are discovered only by the next poll,
+that poll's entire body can be voided by two literal no-op catch branches with zero
+observability, the poll is switched off completely inside every game scene, and nothing removes
+the player from the lobby when the app quits. On top of that the UI panel that shows the party
+never re-reads when it opens, and the avatar sentinel `0` resolves to a real icon instead of
+failing.
+
+---
+
+## 2. Confirmed root causes
+
+Ordered by blast radius. All verified in source; `[A]/[B]/[C]` = which symptom it drives.
+
+### RC-1 — No push channel; latency floor is one poll tick `[A][B]`
+
+`Update()` → `LobbyRefreshScheduler.ShouldFireNow` → `RefreshAsync()` →
+`ISession.RefreshAsync()` → `RefreshOnlinePlayersDiff()` (`HostConnectionService.cs:386,1088,1115`).
+No `PlayerJoined` / `PlayerLeaving` / `Changed` / `PlayerPropertiesChanged` subscription exists on
+`IPresenceLobbyService.ActiveLobby`.
+
+*Correction from verification:* the floor is not absolute — `ForceRefreshNow()` (`:880`) is an
+out-of-band trigger called from `FriendsListPanel.OnEnable` (`:90`), `ArcadeLobbyList.OnEnable`
+(`:107`) and `PartyInviteController` (`:228`), and `Boost()` drops the interval to 0.75 s for 15 s
+after invite events. But `ArcadeLobbyList.OnEnable` effectively never fires (RC-9), so that panel
+gets no out-of-band pull at all.
+
+### RC-2 — Two no-op catch branches void the whole tick, silently `[A][B][C]`
+
+`await _lobbyService.RefreshAsync()` is the **first** await in the try (`:1088`). Everything that
+maintains UI state is downstream in the same try: the online diff (`:1115`), invite scan
+(`:1118-1123`), acceptance scan (`:1129`), `ScanPresenceForJoinedPartyMembers` (`:1162`),
+`RefreshPartyMembersAsync` (`:1165`), `PublishPartyStateIfChangedAsync` (`:1167`). If the first
```

</details>

### `44587a2fa` — fix(presence): account for refresh ticks voided by benign SDK faults

_Claude, 2026-08-01 21:17:12 +0000_

```text
Both benign branches in HostConnectionService's two refresh catches were
literally empty - no log, no counter, no state change:

    if (IsBenignLobbyPatcherError(e))
    {
        // intentional: no log, no counter increment, no state change
    }

That silence was deliberate (B1 fired ~every 3s in solo Menu_Main and the
console spam was the reported bug), but it hid a second, larger problem.
The lobby read is the FIRST await inside the try, and every consumer is
downstream of it in the same try - the online-player diff, the invite
scan, the acceptance scan, the joined-member scan, RefreshPartyMembersAsync
and PublishPartyStateIfChangedAsync. So a benign throw does not merely
skip some SDK bookkeeping; it aborts the entire refresh cycle. A voided
tick was byte-for-byte indistinguishable from a healthy one, which is why
a sustained SDK stale-index episode presents as "the online list is stale
and nothing in the console says why."

Adds per-read-path counters (_benignPresenceSkips / _benignPartySessionSkips),
exposed as BenignPresenceSkips / BenignPartySessionSkips for the NetDiag
overlay, and one throttled diagnostic line per 10s carrying both running
counts plus NetworkDiagnostics.ClassifyException + GetSnapshot.

Uses CSDebug.Log, not Debug.LogWarning: CSDebug.Log is compiled out
entirely in release builds via [Conditional], so this restores
observability without restoring the spam B1 was about. One line per 10s
carrying cumulative counts means a burst stays legible from a single line.

This is the data PresenceSystem/REFACTOR.md's LobbyMembershipMonitor
extraction is blocked on ("wait for NetDiag data ... to tell us how often
the existing watchdog escalates falsely vs truly") - that data was
unobtainable while these branches stayed silent.

No behavior change: classification, control flow and recovery are
untouched. Only counters and a throttled editor-only log are added.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-2 (Commit 1 of 8).
```

 Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/HostConnectionService.cs | 99 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++--
 1 file changed, 96 insertions(+), 3 deletions(-)
```

+        /// <summary>
+        /// Minimum seconds between benign-SDK-skip diagnostic lines. The whole
+        /// point of classifying these faults as benign was to stop the console
+        /// spam (<c>Docs/PresenceSystem/BUGS.md</c> B1 fired ~every 3 s in solo
+        /// Menu_Main), so the accounting must stay quiet enough not to reintroduce
+        /// it while still making a sustained stall visible. One line per 10 s

+        /// </summary>

+
         /// <summary>
         /// After session creation, suppress <see cref="RefreshPartyMembersAsync"/>
         /// for this many seconds.  A freshly-provisioned session can transiently

### `11559a934` — fix(party): make the rate-limit classifier chain-walking and shared

_Claude, 2026-08-01 21:20:15 +0000_

```text
Three classes each carried a private IsRateLimitException and no two
agreed, none walked InnerException:

  HostConnectionService  e.Message.Contains("Too Many Requests")
  PresenceLobbyService   e.Message.Contains("Too Many Requests")
  PartySessionService    e is RequestFailedException rfe && rfe.ErrorCode == 429

Each caught a case the others missed, and because UGS and UniTask both
wrap, a wrapped 429 matched none of them - even though the two sibling
classifiers alongside the first one (IsDefiniteSessionGoneException,
IsBenignLobbyPatcherError) do walk the chain.

Consequences, worst first:

- HostConnectionService.RefreshAsync: a missed 429 fell through to the
  generic branch, which increments the counter toward
  MAX_REFRESH_ERRORS_BEFORE_RECONNECT. Being throttled could therefore
  escalate into ForceReset + a throwaway presence lobby - the opposite of
  backing off, and a plausible contributor to the lobby-split symptom.
- PresenceLobbyService: the `catch ... when (IsRateLimitException(qe))`
  filters on query/join/create never fired for a wrapped 429, so the
  2/4/8s retry was skipped and the failure propagated.
- PartySessionService: same for the CreateAsync / JoinByIdAsync retries.

Adds UgsErrorClassifier.IsRateLimit - one chain-walking classifier
matching the union of the three real signals (SessionException with
Error == RateLimited, RequestFailedException with ErrorCode == 429, and
the plain-text "Too Many Requests"). All three sites delegate to it. A
bare "429" substring is deliberately not matched; it appears in session
and player ids. NetworkDiagnostics.ClassifyException is deliberately NOT
reused - it is documented as "for LOGS, not RETRY CONTROL" and merging
them would let a logging tweak change retry behavior.

Also moves the [rate-limit] branch ABOVE the two [benign] branches at
both catch sites. The classifier fix alone was not enough:
IsBenignSdkStaleIndexError matches ANY SessionException whose Error is
Unknown, which is exactly how a wrapped 429 can present, so with benign
first the throttle was still classified as harmless noise and no backoff
was armed. The two are disjoint in practice (a stale-index fault carries
neither a 429 nor "Too Many Requests"), so the reorder takes nothing from
the benign branches.

[definite] deliberately does NOT move above [benign], contrary to what
Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md Commit 1 originally called for.
Verified on re-read: structured definite errors (SessionNotFound /
SessionDeleted / NotInLobby) carry a specific Error and so are never
matched by the Unknown discriminator - they already reach [definite]
correctly today. The only input the current order sends to benign
instead is a SessionException the SDK itself could not classify whose
message merely reads like "session ... not found", and promoting
[definite] would route that ambiguous case into
HandleDefiniteSessionGoneAsync - recreating the solo session and kicking
any client mid-join. "Retry next tick" is the safe reading of an error
the SDK could not name. Rationale recorded at both the branch and the
classifier doc.

Corrects the IsBenignSdkStaleIndexError class doc, which asserted that
"the [definite] / rate-limit branches handle before this check runs" -
an ordering that existed at neither catch site and is how this defect
survived review.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-3 (Commit 1 of 8).
```

 5 files changed, 203 insertions(+), 22 deletions(-)
```

### `92ec00f7a` — fix(presence): NetDiag on every PresenceLobbyService catch

_Claude, 2026-08-01 21:21:43 +0000_

```text
Three of the seven catches in PresenceLobbyService carried a NetDiag
classification line; four did not - and the four that did not are the
ones that matter most for diagnosing a wrong or partial online list:

- ConvergeToCanonicalAsync query catch
- ConvergeToCanonicalAsync join catch
- TryQueryAndJoinAsync per-session join catch
- DeleteOwnLobbyQuietlyAsync catch

Both halves of ConvergeToCanonicalAsync are the self-heal for a
simultaneous-create race. When convergence fails, two clients sit in
different presence lobbies and each renders the other's panel as empty -
the exact reported symptom - and the only evidence was an unclassified
LogWarning that could not distinguish Offline from RateLimit from
SessionGone. That distinction is the input
Docs/PresenceSystem/REFACTOR.md's LobbyMembershipMonitor extraction is
explicitly blocked on ("wait for NetDiag data ... class=Offline vs
class=SessionGone vs class=Transient will tell us what the watchdog
should actually escalate on"), and TODO-P5 asks for a class tally that
could not have been produced from these paths.

Adds a private LogNetDiag(operation, e) helper carrying an operation tag,
uses it at all seven catches, and collapses the three pre-existing
fully-qualified one-liners onto it. The operation tag is new: previously
every NetDiag line in this file was identical apart from the class, so a
log could not tell you WHICH lobby operation failed.

Editor-only: CSDebug.Log is compiled out in release builds.
No behavior change - logging only.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-1 diagnostics /
TODO-P5 (Commit 1 of 8).
```

### `09381defe` — refactor(party): decouple backoff and session-settle from the poll interval

_Claude, 2026-08-01 21:46:38 +0000_

```text
refreshIntervalSeconds was doing three jobs. Two of them were incidental:

  _rateLimitBackoffUntil = Time.unscaledTime + refreshIntervalSeconds * 2;  (x2)
  _scheduler.ResetDeferred(refreshIntervalSeconds);                          (x2)

Neither has anything to do with how often we poll. How long to retreat
after UGS says 429 is a property of UGS's throttle, and how long a
freshly provisioned session needs before we touch it is a property of
UGS's provisioning - both were riding the cadence field only because it
happened to be a float in scope.

Replaces them with RATE_LIMIT_BACKOFF_SECONDS = 6f and
POST_SESSION_SETTLE_SECONDS = 3f. Both values are exactly what the old
expressions produced with the shipped prefab (refreshIntervalSeconds: 3),
so this is a rename of two numbers, not a retune.

Sequencing note: this lands BEFORE wiring the cadence field to the
scheduler (next commit). Done in the other order, correcting the prefab
value would silently halve the rate-limit backoff and shorten the session
settle as a side effect of an unrelated fix - three behavior changes
disguised as one.

No behavior change.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-4 (Commit 2 of 8).
```

```text
 Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/HostConnectionService.cs | 40 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++----
 1 file changed, 36 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)
```

diff --git a/Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/HostConnectionService.cs b/Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/HostConnectionService.cs
index ecb6668e4..8bb6a2142 100644
--- a/Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/HostConnectionService.cs
+++ b/Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/HostConnectionService.cs
@@ -107,6 +107,38 @@ namespace CosmicShore.Gameplay
         /// </summary>
         private const float PRESENCE_CONVERGE_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 4f;
 
+        /// <summary>
+        /// How long the whole <see cref="Update"/> loop stays parked after UGS
+        /// returns a 429.
+        ///
+        /// <para>
+        /// Previously computed as <c>refreshIntervalSeconds * 2</c>, which
+        /// coupled two unrelated things: how often we poll and how long we
+        /// retreat when told to stop. The value here (6 s) is exactly what that
+        /// expression produced with the shipped prefab value
+        /// (<c>refreshIntervalSeconds: 3</c>), so this is a rename of a number,
+        /// not a retune - but it means wiring the cadence field to the scheduler
+        /// can no longer silently halve the backoff as a side effect.
+        /// </para>
+        /// </summary>
+        private const float RATE_LIMIT_BACKOFF_SECONDS = 6f;
+
+        /// <summary>
+        /// Extra settling time granted to a freshly created or joined party
+        /// session before the first member-sync refresh touches it, ON TOP of the
+        /// normal refresh interval (see <see cref="LobbyRefreshScheduler.ResetDeferred"/>).
+        /// Guards against stale-session 404s against a session UGS has only just
+        /// provisioned.
+        ///
+        /// <para>
+        /// Also previously passed as <c>refreshIntervalSeconds</c>; 3 s preserves
+        /// the shipped prefab value. Same rationale as
+        /// <see cref="RATE_LIMIT_BACKOFF_SECONDS"/> - the settle exists because

### `084dce0ba` — fix(presence): make refreshIntervalSeconds actually drive the refresh

_Claude, 2026-08-01 21:47:31 +0000_

```text
The inspector field named "How often (seconds) to refresh the online
player list and check for invites" did not control that. The scheduler is
DI-constructed by a factory that cannot see the component's serialized
config:

    // 1.5f matches the HCS SerializeField default (refreshIntervalSeconds).
    builder.RegisterFactory(_ => new LobbyRefreshScheduler(1.5f), ...);

That comment was true when written and rotted when PartyServices.prefab
was overridden to 3. From then on the two numbers were independent and
the hardcoded 1.5 silently won, so the shipped poll cadence was 1.5s
while the inspector claimed 3s - and anyone tuning the field changed only
the backoff and settle timings that (until the previous commit) also rode
it.

Adds LobbyRefreshScheduler.DefaultInterval and assigns it from
HostConnectionService.Start. The prefab moves 3 -> 1.5 so the EFFECTIVE
cadence is unchanged at 1.5s; this commit makes the field honest, it does
not retune the poll. Deliberately not left at 3: wiring it up while
keeping 3 would halve the poll rate and make the staleness this branch is
fixing measurably worse, with no compensating mechanism until the push
channel lands in Commit 4. The field becomes the safety-poll knob (10s
menu / 30s in game) at that point.

The factory comment now says what the argument is - a placeholder that
Start overwrites - so the next reader does not treat it as the tuning
point again.

No behavior change.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-4 (Commit 2 of 8).
```

### `6a3a37a57` — fix(presence): measure wall time, jitter the interval, stop breaching the read cap

_Claude, 2026-08-01 21:49:05 +0000_

```text
Three fixes to LobbyRefreshScheduler, all about the poll landing when it
should.

1. The accumulator measured the wrong clock. ShouldFireNow(dt) both
   accumulated and consumed, so it could only be called after the caller's
   eligibility gates:

       if (_lobbyMutex.CurrentCount == 0) return;   // a write holds this
       ...
       if (_scheduler.ShouldFireNow(Time.unscaledDeltaTime))

   Time spent behind those gates did not accumulate. The mutex is held for
   the duration of every property write - which costs a pre-write refresh,
   a save, a post-save refresh, plus 2s per retry - so the clock stopped
   for seconds at a time, and stopped precisely when a write was in
   flight, i.e. when party state was CHANGING. The next poll then landed a
   further full interval after the write completed. The refresh loop went
   blind exactly when the roster was moving.

   Split into Accumulate(dt), called unconditionally at the top of Update
   before any gate, and TryConsumeFire(), called after them. A tick
   blocked by a gate now keeps its accumulated time and fires as soon as
   it becomes eligible. It cannot burst: the accumulator zeroes on
   consume, so a long ineligible stretch yields exactly one catch-up fire.

   Intended side effect: the timer now also accumulates during boot, so
   the first online-list population fires immediately on lobby join
   instead of one interval later. Session creation still gets its settle
   window via ResetDeferred.

2. Adds +/-10% per-fire jitter (TODO-P3). Every client polled on an
   identical cadence, and clients mostly start in bursts - a party
   launching together, an MPPM run - so their reads arrived at UGS in
   phase, concentrating load into spikes and raising 429 incidence for
   everyone simultaneously. Re-rolled per fire, not per client, so two
   clients that drift into phase do not stay there. Median cadence
   unchanged.

3. BOOSTED_INTERVAL_SECONDS 0.75f -> 1.1f. Its own comment claimed 0.75s
   "keeps us safely under" the ~1/s UGS lobby read cap; 0.75s is 1.33
   reads/s, over it - and the boost window overlaps the invite handshake,
   the busiest stretch for property writes. 1.1s is 0.91 reads/s, leaving
   room for the 4s converge query on top. Costs ~0.35s of worst-case
   invite latency; a 429 parks the whole Update loop for
   RATE_LIMIT_BACKOFF_SECONDS (6s), so tripping the cap to save a third of
   a second costs roughly seventeen times that in blindness.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-4 / RC-13, TODO-P3
(Commit 2 of 8).
```

### `b0adfa728` — feat(presence): subscribe the presence lobby's ISession push events

_Claude, 2026-08-01 21:58:10 +0000_

```text
The presence lobby has always been an ISession, and this codebase already
consumes ISession events on the PARTY session (PartySessionService
subscribes PlayerLeaving at create and join). The presence lobby was
simply never subscribed - so every arrival and departure anywhere in the
world was discovered only by the next poll tick. This is the missing
push half of RC-1.

Event names and signatures verified against Unity's API reference for
com.unity.services.multiplayer@1.1 (the package is not vendored in this
checkout, so they could not be confirmed from source):

  PlayerJoined            Action<string>
  PlayerLeaving           Action<string>
  PlayerHasLeft           Action<string>
  PlayerPropertiesChanged Action
  Changed                 Action
  RemovedFromSession      Action
  Deleted                 Action

Adds ConsumeRosterDirty() and ConsumeMembershipLost() to
IPresenceLobbyService. Roster-affecting pushes set one flag; the two
definite membership-loss pushes set another, because the recoveries
differ - a roster change means re-read, a membership loss means rejoin.
Keeping them separate also means the membership signal is authoritative
(the service telling us we are out) rather than the error-count heuristic
the reconnect path uses today, so it can be acted on without the NetDiag
data that PresenceSystem/REFACTOR.md's LobbyMembershipMonitor extraction
is gated on.

The handlers do exactly one thing: Interlocked-set an int. No Unity API,
no SOAP raise, no logging. The SDK gives no thread guarantee for its
dispatch, and per Docs/THREADING.md a SOAP Raise() invokes listeners
INLINE - so raising from a push handler would run FriendsListPanel's
Instantiate/Destroy off the main thread and throw
EnsureRunningOnMainThread. Draining the flag from Update() is both the
main-thread guarantee and a per-frame coalescer: a four-player join burst
produces one refresh, not four. The file's THREAD SAFETY header now
records this as the one deliberate exception.

Subscription is routed through a single SetActiveLobby(next) choke point
rather than wire/unwire calls beside each of the six assignment sites
(initial join, create, converge migration, leave, quiet delete,
force-reset). Missing one would either leak a subscription onto a session
we no longer hold - marking the roster dirty from a lobby we left - or
leave a freshly joined lobby with no push, silently reverting that path
to poll-only. Handlers are named methods, not lambdas, so -= actually
removes them.

Note on PlayerLeaving: UGS documents it as firing BEFORE the session
updates, so the departing player is still in Players at callback time.
That is exactly why this sets a flag rather than reading the roster
inline - the drain runs a frame later, after the session has updated.

No consumer yet; HostConnectionService picks these up in the next commit.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-1 (Commit 4 of 8).
```

+        /// Returns <c>true</c> (and clears the flag) when the UGS session has

+        /// departure anywhere in the world was discovered only by the next poll
+        /// tick. Callers should treat a <c>true</c> return as "refresh now"
+        /// while keeping their periodic poll as a safety net.
+        /// </para>
+        ///
+        /// <para>
+        /// Deliberately a polled flag rather than a C# event: the SDK raises its
+        /// callbacks from its own dispatch context, and raising a SOAP event or
+        /// touching a <c>UnityEngine.Object</c> from there would run listeners
+        /// inline off the main thread (see <c>Docs/THREADING.md</c>). Draining
+        /// the flag from <c>Update()</c> is both the main-thread guarantee and a
+        /// per-frame coalescer - a four-player join burst produces one refresh,
+        /// not four.
+        /// </para>
+        /// </summary>
+        bool ConsumeRosterDirty();
+
+        /// <summary>
+        /// Returns <c>true</c> (and clears the flag) when UGS has pushed a
+        /// DEFINITE membership-loss signal since the last call - either
+        /// <c>RemovedFromSession</c> (we were removed) or <c>Deleted</c> (the
+        /// lobby is gone).
+        ///
+        /// <para>
+        /// Distinct from <see cref="ConsumeRosterDirty"/> because the recovery is
+        /// different: a roster change means re-read, a membership loss means
+        /// rejoin. This is an authoritative statement from the service, not the
+        /// error-count heuristic
+        /// (<c>MAX_REFRESH_ERRORS_BEFORE_RECONNECT</c>) the reconnect path
+        /// otherwise relies on - so it can be acted on without waiting for the
+        /// NetDiag data that <c>Docs/PresenceSystem/REFACTOR.md</c>'s
+        /// <c>LobbyMembershipMonitor</c> extraction is gated on.
+        /// </para>
+        /// </summary>
+        bool ConsumeMembershipLost();
     }
 }

### `8a1467958` — feat(presence): drive the refresh off the push channel

_Claude, 2026-08-01 22:00:20 +0000_

```text
HostConnectionService now consumes the ISession push flags added in the
previous commit:

- ConsumeRosterDirty() -> refresh immediately instead of waiting out the
  remaining poll interval, and Reset() the scheduler so the safety poll
  re-arms from now rather than firing a redundant read right behind the
  push. This is the change that turns "a player arrived, discovered on
  some later tick" into "delivered when it happens".
- ConsumeMembershipLost() -> rejoin immediately. RemovedFromSession /
  Deleted are UGS stating the fact, not the consecutive-error heuristic
  the existing watchdog infers it from, so there is no reason to wait for
  three refreshes to fail against a lobby that no longer exists.

The periodic poll is deliberately LEFT at its current cadence rather than
being relaxed to the planned 10s safety interval. Push is unverified in
the editor (this checkout cannot compile), so until it is confirmed
working the poll stays as the load-bearing path and push is purely
additive - strictly faster, no regression if it never fires. Relaxing the
interval is a prefab-only change once verified, and is where the
rate-limit budget gets reclaimed.

Two fixes pulled forward because this commit adds a caller that would
otherwise widen them:

1. ApplyPostLobbyJoinState no longer clears a live party roster.
   SeedLocalPlayer(clearFirst: true) was unconditional, so any presence
   rejoin - reconnect, converge migration, and now a pushed membership
   loss - wiped every remote party member even though NetworkManager, the
   session and the members themselves were untouched. ScriptableList.Clear()
   raises only OnCleared and never per-item OnItemRemoved, so
   ArcadeLobbyList blanked slots 1-3 and disabled the Leave button while
   the party was perfectly alive. Now clears only when there is no live
   session, which on the initial join is still the case (the session is
   created after this runs), so first-boot behaviour is unchanged. This is
   RC-8, scheduled for Commit 6; adding a third caller to a known
   roster-wiping path without fixing it was not defensible.

2. SeedLocalPlayer is now idempotent. With clearFirst:false the list can
   already hold our row, and PartyPlayerData keys Equals/GetHashCode on
   PlayerId alone, so a blind Add would leave two entries for the same
   player - rendering as the local player occupying two arcade slots.

Also the first call site in the codebase to read PartyStateMachine.
TryTransition's return value: Reconnecting is only legal from Inviting /
JoiningParty / InParty, so the recovery only transitions back out when it
actually got in. On success it leaves Reconnecting for InParty rather
than stranding the machine there (it has no other exit today); with no
live session it raises OnHostConnectionLost so the user-driven retry can
recreate it, deliberately not calling EnsurePartySessionAsync from this
background path - that would Shutdown() NetworkManager and respawn every
menu vessel, the same reason the consecutive-error path avoids it.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-1, RC-8 (Commit 4 of 8).
```

 2 files changed, 94 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)
```

diff --git a/Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/HostConnectionService.cs b/Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/HostConnectionService.cs
index 24a3feab9..8061aecff 100644
--- a/Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/HostConnectionService.cs
+++ b/Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/HostConnectionService.cs
@@ -472,13 +472,77 @@ namespace CosmicShore.Gameplay
 
             ExpireOutgoingInvites();
 
+            // UGS told us we are no longer in the lobby (RemovedFromSession /
+            // Deleted). Unlike the consecutive-error watchdog below in
+            // RefreshAsync, this is not a heuristic - it is the service stating
+            // the fact - so recover immediately instead of waiting for three
+            // refreshes to fail against a lobby that no longer exists.
+            if (_lobbyService.ConsumeMembershipLost())
+            {
+                HandlePresenceMembershipLostAsync().Forget();
+                return;
+            }
+
+            // Push: a roster-affecting event arrived since the last frame.
+            // Refresh now rather than waiting out the remaining interval - this
+            // is what turns "discovered on the next tick" into "delivered when it
+            // happens". Reset the scheduler so the safety poll re-arms from now
+            // and we do not immediately follow the push with a redundant read.
+            bool pushed = _lobbyService.ConsumeRosterDirty();
+            if (pushed) _scheduler.Reset();
+
             // Consume only once eligible, so a tick blocked by the gates keeps its
             // accumulated time and fires as soon as it can rather than restarting
             // the interval. Cannot burst - the accumulator zeroes on consume.
-            if (_scheduler.TryConsumeFire())
+            if (pushed || _scheduler.TryConsumeFire())
                 RefreshAsync().Forget();
         }
 
+        /// <summary>
+        /// Recovers from a definite presence-lobby membership loss pushed by UGS
+        /// (<c>RemovedFromSession</c> or <c>Deleted</c>).
+        ///
+        /// <para>
+        /// Deliberately does NOT touch the party session, NetworkManager or any
+        /// vessel: the presence lobby is the discovery layer and is independent
+        /// of the Relay-backed party. Losing it means peers stop appearing in the
+        /// online list, not that the party broke. This is the same reason
+        /// <see cref="ApplyPostLobbyJoinState"/> must not clear a live party
+        /// roster on rejoin.
+        /// </para>
+        /// </summary>
+        private async UniTaskVoid HandlePresenceMembershipLostAsync()
+        {
+            Debug.LogWarning("[HostConnectionService] Presence lobby membership lost (UGS push) - rejoining.");
+            CSDebug.Log($"[HostConnectionService] NetDiag: {NetworkDiagnostics.GetSnapshot()}");
+
+            // Reconnecting is only legal from Inviting / JoiningParty / InParty,
+            // so capture whether we actually entered it - this is the first call
+            // site in the codebase to READ TryTransition's return value, and it is
+            // what stops the recovery below from firing an illegal
+            // <whatever> → InParty when we never left the original state.
+            bool enteredReconnecting = _stateMachine.TryTransition(PartyState.Reconnecting);
+
+            _lobbyService.ForceReset();
+            await _lobbyService.JoinOrCreateAsync(presenceLobbyMaxPlayers);
+            ApplyPostLobbyJoinState();
+
+            // Leave Reconnecting rather than stranding the machine there (it has
+            // no other exit today). The presence lobby is independent of the
+            // Relay party, so if the session is still live we are fully
+            // recovered. If it is not, surface the loss and let the explicit
+            // user-driven retry recreate it - recreating a session from this
+            // background path would Shutdown() NetworkManager and respawn every
+            // menu vessel, which is exactly why the consecutive-error reconnect
+            // path in RefreshAsync does not do it either.
+            if (!enteredReconnecting) return;
+
+            if (_partySessionService.ActiveSession != null)
+                _stateMachine.TryTransition(PartyState.InParty);
+            else
+                _eventBus.RaiseHostConnectionLost();
+        }
+
```

### `2452a3922` — docs(presence): correct the property-writer retry comment; pin both refreshes

_Claude, 2026-08-01 22:01:13 +0000_

```text
SaveWithRetryAsync's doc said "exponential retry" and the inline comment
said "Back off exponentially", while the code waits a fixed baseDelayMs
every attempt. Someone reading either would have concluded the backoff
was already exponential.

Documents that the fixed delay is now a deliberate choice, not an
oversight: the retry loop holds LobbyMutex, and that mutex also gates
HostConnectionService.Update's refresh path, so stretching three retries
from ~6s to ~14s would widen the window in which the roster cannot be
re-read - to fix a rate-limit problem that the push channel and the
interval work already reduced. Revisit only with real 429 counts
(TODO-P5) showing the retries saturating.

Also pins both surrounding RefreshAsync calls against the deletion
PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md originally scheduled here. The plan justified
removing them with "the push channel delivers our own change back", which
does not hold: UGS documents PlayerPropertiesChanged as NOT firing when
the properties are already up to date locally, which is exactly the case
for our own write. The pre-write refresh guards a stale player-index that
makes SaveCurrentPlayerDataAsync fail SILENTLY; the post-save refresh
re-syncs the SDK cache so later WebSocket deltas do not reference stale
indices, which is B1's root cause. Removing them to save UGS calls would
likely make B1 worse.

Comments only - no behavior change.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-13 (Commit 4 of 8).
```

### `52b8f5f65` — fix(presence): leave the lobby explicitly on quit and background

_Claude, 2026-08-01 22:04:49 +0000_

```text
The only presence-lobby leave outside sign-out was an async void
OnDestroy awaiting LeaveAsync after teardown had already begun. Unity
does not wait for async void during shutdown, so the UGS request usually
never completed - the file's own error text admitted the consequence:
"other users may see this player online for ~30s until UGS reaps the
entry." There was no OnApplicationQuit, OnApplicationPause or
Application.wantsToQuit handler anywhere in Controller/Party, and no quit
path left the Relay party session at all.

Adds ApplicationLifecycleManager.OnAppQuitRequested (static event + SOAP
channel + authored asset, wired into ApplicationLifecycleEvents.asset).
It is raised from an Application.wantsToQuit handler that returns false
ONCE, holds the quit open for QUIT_DRAIN_SECONDS (1.5s), then re-requests
it and lets it through. Returning false is what makes an awaited UGS
leave possible at all - the app keeps running normally during the window.
Distinct from the existing OnAppQuitting, which fires from
OnApplicationQuit and is structurally too late for anything async.

HostConnectionService subscribes and leaves, bounded to 1200ms so a hung
request can never outlive the drain window:

- QUIT: leaves BOTH the presence lobby and the Relay party session. We
  are not coming back, so neither should wait on a reap timer. This is
  the one case where sub-second removal on every peer is actually
  achievable - UGS fans an explicit leave out over the WebSocket, so
  peers see it on their next drained push.
- PAUSE(true): leaves the PRESENCE LOBBY ONLY, keeping the party. A
  backgrounded app may never be resumed (the OS can kill it silently,
  which is exactly the ghost-row case), but a three-second app switch
  must not eject the player from their party. Netcode/Relay has real
  transport-level disconnect handling for that; the presence lobby has
  nothing but the reap timer, which is why only it needs this.
- PAUSE(false): rejoins. JoinOrCreateAsync is idempotent.

The pause path deliberately does NOT mark the state machine Disconnected.
IsInitialized is derived from it, so doing so would make the resume
branch conclude we were never initialized and silently never rejoin,
leaving the player invisible for the rest of the session. Tracked with a
separate _leftPresenceForBackground flag instead.

OnDestroy keeps its leave as a backstop for paths that skip the lifecycle
hooks (scene-level Destroy, duplicate-instance teardown), now guarded on
ActiveLobby != null so a normal quit does not double-leave.

Honest scope: this covers the quit button, alt-F4, mobile backgrounding
and (in most Unity versions) stopping play mode. A hard kill, a crash or
an OS termination never reaches any of these hooks - for those the UGS
reap window remains the floor, which is what the tombstone work in
Commit 6 is for.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-5 (Commit 3 of 8).
```

### `75b8912e4` — docs(presence): record commits 3+4, add the batched verification guide

_Claude, 2026-08-01 22:06:45 +0000_

```text
Marks Commits 3 and 4 shipped in PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md and adds
PRESENCE_SYNC_VERIFICATION.md - a single step-by-step guide covering all
of commits 1-4 in one editor pass, since verification is being batched
rather than run per-commit.

The guide is ordered cheapest-failure-first (compile + hand-authored
asset import, then solo baseline, then 2-VP push, then 3-VP party smoke,
then quit/background, then rate limits) and calls out which observations
are informative in BOTH directions - notably that a benign-skip counter
staying at zero would mean B1 is not the staleness cause and would
re-weight the remaining commits.

Also records the Commit 4 deviation as a rejection rather than another
deferral: the LobbyPropertyWriter write-reduction is not doable as
planned, because UGS documents PlayerPropertiesChanged as not firing when
properties are already up to date locally - which is exactly our own
write - so push cannot stand in for the post-save refresh.

Two follow-ups are explicitly gated on verification results: relaxing the
safety poll 1.5 -> 10 (prefab-only, where the rate-limit budget is
actually reclaimed) and reporting the benign-skip counter.
```

-### Commit 3 — Explicit leave on quit / pause **[RC-5]**
-

-  → `PresenceService.EnterDeparting()` → bounded `UniTask.WhenAny(leave, Delay(1200))` over
-  `_lobbyService.LeaveAsync()` **and** `_partySessionService.LeaveAsync()` (no quit path leaves the
-  Relay session today). On mobile, `OnAppPaused(true)` is the only hook that ever runs — leave there
-  and rejoin on `OnAppPaused(false)`.
-- `OnDestroy` — keep the leave as a backstop, guarded by `CurrentState != Offline` so the quit path
-  doesn't double-leave.
-
-### Commit 4 — Push channel + membership monitor **[RC-1; ARCH]**
-
-- `PresenceLobbyService` — `WireSessionEvents` / `UnwireSessionEvents` per § 4.4; expose
-  `ConsumeRosterDirty()` / `ConsumeMembershipDirty()`.
-- New `LobbyMembershipMonitor` — `{ Active, StaleReference, RemovedFromLobby, LobbyDeleted }`, fed by
-  push + refresh outcomes. **Replaces `MAX_REFRESH_ERRORS_BEFORE_RECONNECT` as the reconnect
-  trigger** (`PresenceSystem/REFACTOR.md`'s extraction, now unblocked by Commit 1's data).
-- `HostConnectionService.Update()` — fire on `ConsumeRosterDirty()` **or** safety-poll elapsed
-  (10 s menu / 30 s game).
-- `:1211-1221` — reconnect reads `MembershipState`, not the error counter. Add the missing
-  `Reconnecting → InPresenceLobby` / `→ HostingParty` exits so `Reconnecting` stops being terminal,
-  and add `(Disconnected, HostingParty)` / `(Disconnected, InParty)` to `LegalTransitions` so the
-  boot-status retry path stops performing two rejected transitions.
+### Commit 3 — Explicit leave on quit/pause **[RC-5]** — ✅ SHIPPED
+
+`52b8f5f6`. `ApplicationLifecycleManager.OnAppQuitRequested` (static event + SOAP
+channel + authored `EventOnAppQuitRequested.asset`, wired into
+`ApplicationLifecycleEvents.asset`), raised from an `Application.wantsToQuit`
+handler that returns `false` once, holds the quit for `QUIT_DRAIN_SECONDS` (1.5 s),
+then re-requests and lets it through. `HostConnectionService` leaves, bounded to
+1200 ms:
+
+| Trigger | Presence lobby | Party session |
+|---|---|---|
+| Quit | leave | leave |
+| Pause (background) | leave | **keep** |
+| Resume | rejoin | — |
+
+Pause keeps the party deliberately: a backgrounded app may never resume (OS kill —
+the ghost-row case), but a three-second app switch must not eject the player from
+their party. Netcode/Relay has real transport-level disconnect handling; the
+presence lobby has only the reap timer, which is why only it needs this.
+
+The pause path must **not** mark the state machine `Disconnected` — `IsInitialized`
+derives from it, so doing so makes the resume branch conclude we were never
+initialized and silently never rejoin. Tracked with `_leftPresenceForBackground`.
+
+`OnDestroy` keeps its leave as a backstop for paths that skip the lifecycle hooks,
+now guarded on `ActiveLobby != null` so a normal quit does not double-leave.
```

</details>

### `24a9b4209` — fix(ui): re-read the party/friends panels every time the modal opens

_Claude, 2026-08-01 22:30:28 +0000_

```text
ArcadeLobbyList did its entire enable-time bootstrap - SubscribeSoap,
PopulateAll, and a ForceRefreshNow() pull of fresh lobby state - in
OnEnable. But the arcade panel lives inside a ModalWindowManager, which
hides a modal by fading its CanvasGroup and NEVER calls SetActive(false)
(DisableWindow() sets alpha/blocksRaycasts/interactable to 0; Start's own
comment says "Parent containers stay active so OnEnable/OnDisable
lifecycle fires for all children"). The GameObject and its whole ancestor
chain ship m_IsActive: 1 in Menu_Main.

So OnEnable fired exactly ONCE per scene load and never again when the
player actually opened the panel. From that first frame on, the panel
never re-read anything: every write to HostConnectionDataSO that does not
raise a SOAP event was invisible to it forever - and LocalDisplayName /
LocalAvatarId are plain field assignments in SyncLocalIdentity, which is
why slot 0 could sit on a stale name or avatar indefinitely.

Adds IModalPanel { OnModalOpened, OnModalClosed }, dispatched by
ModalWindowManager from ModalWindowIn/ModalWindowOut. This is parent-to-
child dispatch inside one prefab hierarchy - the same shape as
ScreenSwitcher driving IScreen.OnScreenEnter - not cross-system
communication, so it is deliberately not a SOAP channel.

The panel array resolves lazily on first dispatch rather than in an Awake
override: this class's own EnsureBackdrop doc warns that "a subclass
declaring its own Awake would silently skip a base Awake hook", and
ArcadeGameConfigureModal does exactly that (void Awake(), not override) -
which is precisely the modal this dispatch exists to fix. Lazy init
cannot be hidden by a subclass.

Both panels bind through OnEnable/OnDisable AND IModalPanel, because
neither alone is sufficient - IModalPanel alone would miss any scene that
does toggle the object. Subscription is idempotent, but the RE-READ
deliberately is not conditional: guarding it behind the subscribe flag
would reintroduce the exact bug, since OnEnable has already set that flag
at scene load.

Also fixed while here:

- ArcadeLobbyList.HandlePartyChanged / HandlePartyCleared now call
  UpdateOnlineStatus(). The "N Players Online" counter adds 1 for the
  local player when IsConnected, so a party change that flipped
  connection state moved the count with nothing repainting it.
- FriendsListPanel.Show() no longer double-builds. It called
  SetActive(true) - which fires OnEnable and rebuilds everything - and
  then PopulateAll() again unconditionally. Now only builds explicitly
  when the object was already active, where OnEnable does not fire.
- FriendsListPanel re-attempts its HostConnectionService.Instance
  subscription on every open instead of once, so a null singleton at
  scene load no longer detaches it permanently.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-9 (Commit 7 of 8).
```

--- a/Assets/_Scripts/UI/Elements/ArcadeLobbyList.cs

@@ -22,7 +22,7 @@ namespace CosmicShore.UI

-    public class ArcadeLobbyList : MonoBehaviour
+    public class ArcadeLobbyList : MonoBehaviour, IModalPanel

@@ -95,9 +95,69 @@ namespace CosmicShore.UI
             }

-        void OnEnable()
+        // ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+        // Visibility lifecycle
+        //
+        // Bound through BOTH OnEnable/OnDisable and IModalPanel, because neither
+        // alone is sufficient:
+        //
+        //   • The arcade panel lives inside a ModalWindowManager, which hides by
+        //     fading its CanvasGroup and never calls SetActive(false). So
+        //     OnEnable fired exactly ONCE per Menu_Main load and never again when
+        //     the player actually opened the panel - meaning the ForceRefreshNow
+        //     pull below, and the full repopulate, simply never happened on open.
+        //     Every write to HostConnectionDataSO that does not raise a SOAP
+        //     event (LocalDisplayName / LocalAvatarId are plain field
+        //     assignments in SyncLocalIdentity) stayed invisible forever.
+        //
+        //   • IModalPanel alone would miss any scene or prefab that DOES toggle
+        //     this GameObject.
+        //
+        // Bind/Unbind are idempotent, so a double open (OnEnable + OnModalOpened
+        // in the same frame on first show) is harmless.
+        // ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+
+        bool _subscribed;
+
+        void OnEnable()  => Bind();
+        void OnDisable() => Unbind();
+
+        /// <inheritdoc/>
+        /// <remarks>
+        /// Subscription is idempotent, but the RE-READ is not conditional: the
+        /// whole point of this hook is that the panel must never render a
+        /// snapshot taken when it was last closed. Guarding the refresh behind
+        /// the subscribe flag would reintroduce exactly the bug this fixes, since
+        /// OnEnable has already set that flag at scene load.
+        /// </remarks>
+        public void OnModalOpened()
+        {
+            Bind();
+            RefreshFromData();
+        }
+
+        /// <inheritdoc/>
+        public void OnModalClosed() => Unbind();
+
+        void Bind()
         {
+            if (_subscribed) return;
+            _subscribed = true;
+
             SubscribeSoap();
+            RefreshFromData();
+        }
+
+        void Unbind()
+        {
+            if (!_subscribed) return;
+            _subscribed = false;
+
+            UnsubscribeSoap();
+        }
+
+        void RefreshFromData()

### `3b9a30fa5` — fix(ui): stop rendering unknown avatars as a real icon

_Claude, 2026-08-01 22:33:30 +0000_

```text
SO_DefaultProfileIcons.asset (the only SO_ProfileIconList in the project)
authors ids 1..18. Every avatar-id PRODUCER defaults to 0:
Player.NetAvatarId is new(0,...), PartyMemberService.ReadMemberData seeds
0, HostConnectionService.ReadOnlinePlayerData seeds 0. And every
CONSUMER fell back to profileIcons[0] on a miss - which IS the icon with
Id 1.

So "unknown" rendered as a real, plausible avatar, indistinguishable from
a player who actually chose icon #1. That is why the reported wrong-icon
symptom was so hard to pin down, and why every other avatar bug behind it
was invisible.

There were NINE duplicated resolvers with three different fallbacks
(profileIcons[0], first-icon-with-a-comment, and none at all).
PartyInviteNotificationPanel had no fallback AND assigned conditionally,
so an unresolvable avatar left the PREVIOUS inviter's face on the invite
prompt - the worst case, since the user decides based on who they think
is asking.

Consolidates all nine onto SO_ProfileIconList.Resolve(id): a lazily built
id->sprite dictionary (replacing a linear scan per row repaint) falling
back to a new authored `unknownIcon`. The resolver lives on the SO itself
rather than in a separate DI-registered service - every consumer already
holds a reference, so no new registration or wiring is needed.

**Assign "Unknown Icon" on SO_DefaultProfileIcons.asset in the editor.**
Left unassigned, unresolved avatars render as nothing, which is still
honest (and DomainAvatarChip.Set keeps its Image enabled so the prefab
placeholder shows) - but a distinct placeholder is the intent.

Also fixed:

- Ids <= 0 are now treated as sentinels, not choices. GetDefaultAvatarId
  and ProfileIconSelectView's "nothing matched" default both use
  FirstSelectableId() so a fresh player is never handed the unknown id as
  their actual avatar, and the picker never offers it.
- ArcadeGameConfigureModal chips now subscribe NetAvatarId.OnValueChanged
  alongside the existing NetDomain subscription. The sprite was sampled
  ONCE at spawn, so any avatar arriving later was dropped permanently -
  and two real paths arrive later: a player joining while the modal is
  open (OnPlayerNetworkSpawnedUlong is gated on Player.IsSpawnReady(),
  which checks name + vessel type but NOT avatar) and any player whose
  cloud profile resolves after spawn via HandleProfileLoadedAfterSpawn.
- CloseAndNotifyClients() now calls DespawnAllChips(). The client close
  path already did; the host path leaked every per-Player NetDomain /
  NetAvatarId handler and the spawn-event watch until the next open, with
  stale handlers firing against destroyed chips.
- ProfileModal's not-ready fallback shows the unknown placeholder instead
  of the first authored icon, which read as "this is your icon".

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-10, RC-11 (Commit 8 of 8).
```

--- a/Assets/_Scripts/ScriptableObjects/SO_ProfileIconList.cs

     public class SO_ProfileIconList : ScriptableObject
     {
         [SerializeField] public List<ProfileIcon> profileIcons;
+
+        [Header("Fallback")]
+        [Tooltip("Shown when an avatar id cannot be resolved - an unset id (0), a profile " +
+                 "that has not loaded yet, or a remote player whose avatar has not replicated. " +
+                 "MUST be visually distinct from every real icon: authored ids start at 1, so " +
+                 "before this existed every resolver fell back to profileIcons[0] and rendered " +
+                 "'unknown' as icon #1 - indistinguishable from a player who actually picked it, " +
+                 "which is why every other avatar bug was invisible. Leave unassigned and " +
+                 "unresolved avatars render as nothing, which is still honest; assigning a " +

+        /// asset has not had one assigned - callers must tolerate that.
+        /// </summary>

+
+        /// <summary>
+        /// Lazily built id → sprite map. Every consumer previously ran its own
+        /// linear scan over the list (there were seven copies, with divergent
+        /// fallbacks), on every row repaint.
+        /// </summary>
+        private Dictionary<int, Sprite> _byId;
+
+        /// <summary>
+        /// Looks up an icon by its authored id.
+        /// </summary>
+        /// <returns><c>true</c> when the id maps to an authored icon.</returns>
+        public bool TryGetIcon(int avatarId, out Sprite sprite)
+        {
+            EnsureCache();
+            return _byId.TryGetValue(avatarId, out sprite) && sprite != null;
+        }
+
+        /// <summary>
+        /// Resolves an avatar id to a sprite, falling back to
+        /// <see cref="UnknownIcon"/>.
+        ///
+        /// <para>
+        /// The single resolution point for the whole project. Do NOT reintroduce
+        /// a local scan with a <c>profileIcons[0]</c> fallback: that silently
+        /// renders every unknown avatar as the first authored icon, so a missing
+        /// or unreplicated avatar looks exactly like a real choice.
+        /// </para>
+        /// </summary>
+        public Sprite Resolve(int avatarId) =>
+            TryGetIcon(avatarId, out var sprite) ? sprite : unknownIcon;
+

### `c9c6db177` — fix(presence): require two absent reads before evicting a player

_Claude, 2026-08-01 22:35:02 +0000_

```text
Both roster diffs removed a player the instant they were missing from a
single read: RefreshOnlinePlayersDiff dropped any id not in the fresh
lobby snapshot, and PartyMemberService.SyncFromSession did the same for
party rows - the latter also raising OnPartyMemberLeft, which flips
Friends presence and forces a full FriendsListPanel rebuild.

One absence is not evidence of departure. A read comes back short for
reasons unrelated to the player: the UGS stale-cache defect (B1/B6), a
refresh landing mid-mutation, or - the code-provable one -
ConvergeToCanonicalAsync swapping _activeLobby mid-cycle so the next diff
runs against a lobby we have only just joined and whose roster has not
populated. The result presented as the whole online panel emptying and
refilling a tick later, and party members vanishing and reappearing:
exactly the reported "wrong info / flickers" symptom.

Both paths now require MISSED_READS_BEFORE_REMOVAL (2) consecutive
absences, with the strike count cleared the moment the player is seen
again so an intermittent connection cannot accumulate strikes across
unrelated blips.

This does NOT slow down a genuine departure in the cases that matter: a
graceful leave arrives as an ISession.PlayerLeaving push (Commit 4) which
drives an immediate re-read, and a party peer dropping hard is caught by
the host's OnClientDisconnect backstop through ReconcilePartyMembersNow.
The extra interval is paid only where there is no push signal at all.

The outgoing-invite cleanup keys off the roster rather than the strike
counter, because the removal loop DELETES a player's counter entry at the
moment it evicts them - reading the counter there would find nothing
precisely when the player had genuinely gone, and the invite would never
clear. "Absent from this read AND no longer in OnlinePlayers" means the
strikes were served.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-6 (Commit 6 of 8).
```

 2 files changed, 111 insertions(+), 12 deletions(-)
```

### `641ec251b` — feat(presence): broadcast "I am in the world" when the menu vessel spawns

_Claude, 2026-08-01 22:40:00 +0000_

```text
Adds PresenceStateMachine - a SIBLING of PartyStateMachine, not an
extension. PartyState answers "which Relay session am I in";
PresenceState answers "what am I doing, and should other people see me".
They are orthogonal: you are simultaneously InParty and InMatch. Folding
them would mean a 7x7 cross-product and re-deriving the 14-edge legal
table the invite handshake depends on.

  Offline -> Joining -> Announced -> Present -> InMatch
                            ^            |         |
                            +--- Recovering <------+
  (-> Departing -> Offline from anywhere)

The requested broadcast is Announced -> Present, driven by
GameDataSO.OnClientReady - raised by ClientPlayerVesselInitializer.
InitializePair for the LOCAL user once its vessel exists, i.e. the moment
the Menu_Main lava-lamp vessel spawns. It publishes a `presenceState`
lobby property, which the Commit 4 push channel delivers to every peer on
their next drained tick.

Announced is the state that fixes the "shows wrong information" half of
the symptom: a peer in the lobby whose vessel has not spawned now renders
as a non-invitable "CONNECTING…" row instead of a normal invitable one.
Inviting them was never going to work - their own refresh loop is not
running yet, so the invite is never scanned for.

Also fixes RC-7, which made in-match status impossible to publish at all.
ResolveCurrentMatchName began with `if (IsOnMenuScene()) return "";`, and
its only trigger, HandleGameLaunch, fires on GameDataSO.OnLaunchGame -
which SceneLoader.LaunchGame also handles - so it ran while Menu_Main was
still active. With the lobby mutex uncontended SemaphoreSlim.WaitAsync
completes synchronously, so the publish executed inline, still on the
menu, returned empty, matched _publishedMatchName, and the change-gate
skipped the write entirely. Then Update's IsOnMenuScene gate killed the
loop for the rest of the match. Net: matchName was NEVER published,
OnlineInfoEntry.Status.InMatch was dead code, and players in a match
rendered as idle and invitable. It now keys off the state machine, and
HandleGameLaunch transitions to InMatch BEFORE publishing.

Compatibility: an absent `presenceState` property parses as Present, not
0/Offline. A peer running a build from before this property existed
publishes nothing, and defaulting to 0 would have made every such player
invisible.

Notes:
- presenceState rides the SAME single SaveWithRetryAsync as the other
  identity fields - one UpdatePlayer call, not one per field.
- ApplyPostLobbyJoinState carries a catch-up probe: OnClientReady is a
  one-shot with no replay, and on a RE-join (reconnect, resume, pushed
  membership loss) the vessel already exists and will not spawn again, so
  waiting for the event would strand us in Announced and show this player
  as "CONNECTING…" to everyone for the rest of the session.
- PresenceStateMachine.TryTransition's return value IS checked at call
  sites, unlike the party machine's, where ignoring it is how a rejected
  transition could leave the system asserting a state it never entered.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-7 + § 4 (Commit 5 of 8).
```

 Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/StateMachine/PresenceStateMachine.cs | 124 ++++++++++++++++++++++
 .../Controller/Party/StateMachine/PresenceStateMachine.cs.meta        |  11 ++
 .../ScriptableObjects/SOAP/ScriptablePartyData/PartyPlayerData.cs     |  40 ++++++-

### `53ab63ddb` — docs(presence): record commits 5-8, rewrite the verification guide for all 8

_Claude, 2026-08-01 22:42:43 +0000_

```text
All 8 commits of the presence-sync branch are now landed and none are
editor-verified. PRESENCE_SYNC_VERIFICATION.md is rewritten to cover the
whole branch in one editor pass, ordered cheapest-failure-first and
oriented around the stated goal - ArcadeLobbyList and FriendsListPanel
showing correct members and info - rather than around commit boundaries.

Calls out the two things that CANNOT be done headlessly and that make the
branch look broken until done: assigning Unknown Icon on
SO_DefaultProfileIcons.asset (no sprite could be authored), and
confirming the raw-YAML wiring of On App Quit Requested took.

Records the deviations from the plan in commits 5-8 so they are not
re-derived:

- PresenceState ships with a C# OnStateChanged rather than a SOAP triple,
  matching PartyStateMachine's established shape in the same subsystem,
  so no further SOAP assets had to be hand-authored.
- The avatar resolver lives ON SO_ProfileIconList rather than as a
  separate DI-registered service - every consumer already holds a
  reference, so it needed no registration, no asset and no AppManager
  change.
- Tombstone RENDERING (dimmed Unconfirmed rows) is not implemented, only
  the eviction delay.
- Granular roster SOAP events were not added; panels still use
  ScriptableList item events.
- LobbyMembershipMonitor remains unextracted, still gated on the
  benign-skip data that Step 1c collects.

Also documents why the benign-skip counter reading ZERO is the more
interesting outcome: it would mean B1 is not the staleness cause and
would re-weight what was worth doing.
```

### `a510bd512` — fix(presence): stuck-at-CONNECTING, and instant removal on explicit leave

_Claude, 2026-08-01 23:37:30 +0000_

```text
Two bugs from the 4-instance MPPM run.

--- Bug 1: three of four instances stuck showing CONNECTING --------------

Announced -> Present depended on CATCHING GameDataSO.OnClientReady, a
one-shot with no replay. There is no ordering guarantee between the
presence-lobby join and the menu vessel spawn, and the two race
differently on the main editor instance than on an MPPM virtual player.
When the signal arrived while the machine was still in Joining, the
transition was ILLEGAL - TryTransition logged a warning, returned false,
and DISCARDED the only signal that would ever come. The player stayed
Announced and rendered as CONNECTING… on every peer for the whole
session. Exactly one instance won that race, which is why one showed
online and three did not.

Fixes:

- HandleLocalVesselReady now LATCHES _localVesselReady unconditionally
  before attempting the transition, so a signal arriving in a state that
  cannot accept it is remembered rather than lost.
- New ReconcilePresenceState() runs each refresh tick and re-derives the
  state from the observable CONDITION (does the local vessel exist?)
  rather than from a caught event, so a missed, early or out-of-order
  signal self-heals within one interval. A state machine must never
  depend on having caught a one-shot.

Two related holes found while tracing it, each able to reproduce the same
symptom on its own:

- BuildLivePresenceProperties did not carry presenceState. It is stateful
  and must survive a rejoin exactly like invite_payloads / joined_party /
  matchName. A converge migration rebuilds the player record from
  BuildLocalPlayerProperties - which knows nothing about presence - and
  silently dropped the key. Same class of bug as BUGS.md B4, which is
  what LivePropertySource exists to prevent; a new stateful key was added
  without registering it there.
- PublishPartyStateIfChangedAsync's change-gate compared against trackers
  describing what we last wrote to a DIFFERENT lobby, so after a
  migration it concluded nothing had changed and never re-published,
  masking any dropped key permanently. The trackers are now invalidated
  when the lobby id changes, which self-heals such a loss whatever caused
  it.

--- Bug 2: ~30s to remove a player who was turned off -------------------

The push handlers for PlayerLeaving / PlayerHasLeft threw away their
payload - the departing player's id - and only set the generic
roster-dirty flag. Removal then fell through to "absent from N
consecutive reads", so an explicit, instant, authoritative leave was
processed as if it were an ambiguous absence.

PresenceLobbyService now collects those ids and exposes
TryConsumeDepartedPlayerIds. RefreshOnlinePlayersDiff drains them FIRST
and evicts on the spot, bypassing the two-strike rule - which exists for
absences that might be a stale read, not for the service naming the
player. Their outstanding invite is cleared with them. This is the
"instant event mentioning its identity" the protocol already provided and
we simply were not reading.

For that push to be emitted the leaver must actually leave, and stopping
play mode does NOT raise Application.wantsToQuit - so in the editor,
where MPPM lives, the Commit 3 departure path never ran at all.
ApplicationLifecycleManager now also raises OnAppQuitRequested from
EditorApplication.playModeStateChanged / ExitingPlayMode. That exit
cannot be deferred, so the leave is dispatched best-effort rather than
awaited; a dispatched leave still fans out to every peer immediately.

--- Not included ---------------------------------------------------------

An earlier revision of this commit also routed the online-diff self-skip
through ResolveLocalPlayerId, on the theory that a transiently-empty
LocalPlayerId would add us to our own roster and inflate the count. That
was written to explain an online count of 5 with 4 instances, which turned
out to be a typo for 4 - the correct value. With no symptom behind it,
that change was speculative and altered control flow in a per-tick path,
so it is dropped. The underlying concern (the field is documented as
transiently empty, while PartyMemberService already takes a resolved id)
is real but unobserved; left as a follow-up rather than shipped blind.

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md RC-1, RC-6, RC-7.
```

 Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/Services/PresenceLobbyService.cs    |  50 +++++++++++++-

### `efa90bf6b` — docs(presence): record B11/B12 and the MPPM retest steps

_Claude, 2026-08-01 23:38:47 +0000_

```text
Logs the two bugs the 4-instance MPPM run found, with root causes, in
PresenceSystem/BUGS.md as B11 and B12 (B7/B8 were already taken by the
party-side tracker).

B11's lesson is worth keeping: a state machine must never depend on
having CAUGHT a one-shot event. Latch the fact, reconcile the condition.
The give-away was the asymmetry - exactly one of four instances behaved -
which says "race", not "logic error".

Adds a retest section to the verification guide leading with the two
regression checks, and replaces the single departure expectation with the
three real cases (in-game quit, MPPM stop, hard kill) since only the
third is still bounded by the UGS reap.
```
