 > **Working order.** Diagnostics-first. The presence-lobby cluster (B4,
 > B6) is the locked-design area — read `ARCHITECTURE.md` and
@@ -23,6 +25,83 @@ Statuses: 🔴 open · 🟡 investigating · 🟢 fixed (commit) · ⚪ deferred
 
 ---
 
+## B11. Presence stuck at `Announced` — peers render "CONNECTING…" forever — FIXED (unverified)
+
+**Found.** 4-instance MPPM run, 2026-08-01. Every instance flying the lava lamp
+normally. `FriendsListPanel` on each:
+
+```
+Ys1 → Ys2 (connecting)  Ys3 (connecting)  Ys4 (connecting)
+Ys2 → Ys1 (online)      Ys3 (connecting)  Ys4 (connecting)
+Ys3 → Ys1 (online)      Ys2 (connecting)  Ys4 (connecting)
+Ys4 → Ys1 (online)      Ys2 (connecting)  Ys3 (connecting)
+```
+
+Exactly one instance published `Present`; the other three never did. The
+asymmetry is the diagnosis: it is a **race**, and one instance won it.
+
+**Root cause.** `Announced → Present` depended on catching
+`GameDataSO.OnClientReady`, a one-shot `ScriptableEventNoParam` with no replay.
+There is no ordering guarantee between the presence-lobby join and the menu
+vessel spawn, and the two race differently on the main editor instance than on
+an MPPM virtual player. When the signal landed while the machine was still in
+`Joining`, `Joining → Present` was illegal — `TryTransition` warned, returned
+`false`, and **discarded the only signal that would ever arrive**.
+
+**Fix** (`a510bd51`). Two parts, both needed:
+1. `HandleLocalVesselReady` latches `_localVesselReady` unconditionally *before*
+   attempting the transition, so a signal arriving in a state that cannot accept
+   it is remembered.
+2. `ReconcilePresenceState()` runs each refresh tick and re-derives the state
+   from the observable **condition** (does the local vessel exist?) rather than
+   from a caught event.
+
+**Lesson.** A state machine must never depend on having *caught* a one-shot.
+Latch the fact, reconcile the condition.
+
+**Two related holes found while tracing it**, each able to reproduce the same
+symptom independently:
+- `BuildLivePresenceProperties` did not carry `presenceState`, so a converge
+  migration rebuilt the player record without it. Same class as B4 — a new
+  stateful key added without registering it with `LivePropertySource`.
+- `PublishPartyStateIfChangedAsync`'s change-gate compared against trackers
+  describing a *different* lobby, so after a migration it never re-published,
+  masking the dropped key permanently. Trackers are now invalidated on lobby-id
+  change.
+
+---
+
+## B12. Explicit leave took ~30 s to remove the player — FIXED (unverified)
+
+**Found.** Same run. Turning off Ys2 left its row on Ys1/Ys3/Ys4 for ~30 s (the
+UGS reap window) before it disappeared.
+
+**Root cause — two independent failures stacked:**
+
+1. **The id was thrown away.** The `PlayerLeaving` / `PlayerHasLeft` pushes carry
+   the departing player's id as their payload. The handlers ignored it and only
+   set the generic roster-dirty flag, so removal fell through to "absent from N
+   consecutive reads" — processing an explicit, authoritative, instant leave as
+   if it were an ambiguous absence.
+2. **The leaver never left.** Stopping play mode does **not** raise
+   `Application.wantsToQuit`, so in the editor — where MPPM lives — the
+   departure path added in Commit 3 never ran, and no push was emitted at all.
+
+**Fix** (`a510bd51`). `PresenceLobbyService` collects departed ids and exposes
```

</details>

### `d6a7b41dd` — docs(presence): cost out the liveness heartbeat and reject it

_Claude, 2026-08-01 23:53:17 +0000_

```text
Answers "is a lastSeen heartbeat cheap at our scale?" properly rather
than by assertion. Short answer: no, and the private friend list makes
the question moot.

The decisive figure is one I had not accounted for anywhere: UGS caps
PLAYER DATA VALUES AT 10 PER PLAYER, and we already use 9 (8 from
BuildLocalPlayerProperties plus presenceState added on this branch). A
lastSeen key spends the last slot in the cap on a keepalive, leaving zero
headroom for any future presence field.

The rest of the costing:

- Interval vs latency: the heartbeat only beats the ~30s reap if the
  staleness threshold is under 30s. At a comfortable 10s interval the
  threshold lands at ~25s - no gain. Useful detection (~5s) needs a ~2s
  interval, and that is where it gets expensive.
- Fanout is O(N^2) in aggregate: every member's beat is pushed to every
  other member.
- At 2s it breaches the read cap EVEN AT N=4 as the code stands, so "it's
  cheap at my scale" is not true today. With both optimisations below it
  fits at N=4, but still spends half the write budget on a keepalive and
  sustains the LobbyPatcher delta churn behind B1/B6.

The friend-list direction removes the need outright: UGS Friends presence
is server-tracked and push-based (the service detects offline from the
client's own connection), costs no property slots, and delivers events
for your friends only rather than fanning out over all 100 lobby members.
FriendsServiceFacade already subscribes to PresenceUpdated - nothing
reads it, which is RC-12.

Also records a defect the analysis surfaced, as TODO-P9: the push drain
in HostConnectionService.Update calls RefreshAsync(), issuing a GetLobby
per inbound delta, when the SDK has ALREADY applied the delta locally
before the callback fires. Unity documents PlayerJoined as firing "right
after the session gets updated", and this repo's own
IsBenignLobbyPatcherError exists because LobbyPatcher.ApplyPatchesToLobby
patches local state from deltas. I introduced that in 8a146795 by routing
push through the existing poll-shaped refresh because it was the smallest
diff - it made push correct but not cheap. Removing the fetch is the
largest single read saving available and is what makes relaxing the
safety poll to 10s safe.

No code changed.
```

 Docs/PresenceSystem/PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md     |   4 +-
 Docs/PresenceSystem/TODOS.md                  |  31 +++++++++
 Docs/README.md                                |   4 ++
 4 files changed, 229 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)
```

<details><summary>Patch (code/doc/text files, first 80 of 277 lines)</summary>

```diff
diff --git a/Docs/PresenceSystem/LIVENESS_COST_ANALYSIS.md b/Docs/PresenceSystem/LIVENESS_COST_ANALYSIS.md
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..f6ffa96f3
--- /dev/null
+++ b/Docs/PresenceSystem/LIVENESS_COST_ANALYSIS.md
@@ -0,0 +1,192 @@
+# Liveness cost analysis — should we add a presence heartbeat?
+
+**Question.** A hard-killed client lingers in every peer's online list until the
+UGS lobby reap (~30 s). Should we add a `lastSeen` heartbeat property plus
+peer-side staleness eviction to bound that?
+
+**Answer: no. Don't build it.** It is not cheap even at N=4 for the latency that
+would make it worthwhile, it consumes the last slot in a hard platform cap, and
+the private-friend-list direction removes the need entirely.
+
+Two optimisations that fall out of the analysis **are** worth doing, and one of
+them fixes something I got wrong in Commit 4.
+
+---
+
+## 1. The hard caps
+
+| Cap | Value | Source |
+|---|---|---|
+| Players per lobby | **100** | Unity Support: "What are the limitations of the Lobby service?" |
+| **Player data values per player** | **10** | same |
+| Lobby data values | 20 | same |
+| GetLobby / QueryLobbies | ~1 req/s per player | in-repo comments — **unconfirmed**, the docs rate-limit page is a JS SPA and would not render |
+| UpdatePlayer | ~5 req/5 s per player | same, unconfirmed |
+
+### The decisive one: we are at 9 of 10 player data values
+
+| # | Key | Written by |
+|---|---|---|
+| 1 | `displayName` | `BuildLocalPlayerProperties` |
+| 2 | `avatarId` | ″ |
+| 3 | `partyCount` | ″ |
+| 4 | `partyMax` | ″ |
+| 5 | `matchName` | ″ |
+| 6 | `invite_payloads` | ″ |
+| 7 | `joined_party` | ″ |
+| 8 | `accepted_invite` | ″ |
+| 9 | `presenceState` | `PublishPartyStateIfChangedAsync` (added this branch) |
+| — | **1 slot free** | |
+
+A `lastSeen` key takes the last slot. Every future presence field — region, ping
+band, platform, rich-presence text, party lock state — would then have to
+displace something. **Spending the final slot on a keepalive is the worst
+possible use of it.**
+
+*Mitigation if it were ever built:* pack the timestamp into `presenceState`
+(`"3|1738…"`). Costs no slot, but couples two unrelated fields into one
+parse — and the value then changes every beat, which is what drives §3.
+
+---
+
+## 2. The latency math says the interval must be small
+
+Let **T** = heartbeat interval, **D** = staleness threshold before eviction.
+D must exceed T with margin for a missed beat, so D ≈ 2.5 T. Detection latency ≈ D.
+
+To beat the ~30 s reap at all, D < 30 → T < 12 s. To be *worth building* — say
+5 s detection — **T ≈ 2 s.**
+
+That is the crux: a comfortable 10 s heartbeat gives D ≈ 25 s, which is
+indistinguishable from the reap it was meant to replace. The heartbeat is only
+useful at a cadence that is expensive.
+
+---
+
+## 3. Cost per client at T = 2 s
+
+Writes scale with 1/T. Inbound deltas scale with **(N−1)/T** — every member's
+beat is fanned to every other member, so aggregate traffic is **O(N²)**.
+
+### As the code stands today
+
+- `LobbyPropertyWriter.WriteAsync` costs **3 UGS calls** per write (pre-write
+  `GetLobby`, `UpdatePlayer`, post-save `GetLobby`).
```

</details>

### `402267528` — perf(presence): don't re-fetch the lobby on a push tick

_Claude, 2026-08-02 00:01:11 +0000_

```text
TODO-P9. The push drain called RefreshAsync(), which issued a GetLobby -
so every inbound delta cost a network round-trip on every client. Since
each member's property write fans out to every other member, that was the
dominant read cost at any lobby size, and it put a round-trip between
"the delta arrived" and "the UI updated", which defeats the point of
having a push channel at all.

The fetch is unnecessary: the SDK has already patched the local session
before the callback fires. Unity documents PlayerJoined / PlayerHasLeft
as firing "right after the session gets updated", and
PlayerPropertiesChanged as not firing "if the properties are already up
to date locally" - a statement that only means anything if the SDK
maintains that local copy. This repo carries its own proof:
IsBenignLobbyPatcherError exists precisely because
LobbyPatcher.ApplyPatchesToLobby throws while patching the local lobby
from a WebSocket delta.

RefreshAsync now takes fetchFromServer. Push ticks pass false and do zero
network I/O - one in-memory diff. Poll ticks are unchanged. Also gated to
fetch ticks: the periodic converge (a QuerySessions call - a burst of
pushes must not become a burst of queries) and RefreshPartyMembersAsync
(a different session entirely; a presence delta is no reason to hit it,
and it has its own PlayerLeaving push plus the safety poll).

Two consequences that needed handling rather than inheriting:

- The reconnect watchdog measures CONNECTION health, so only fetch ticks
  may feed it. Push ticks now neither clear nor increment
  _consecutiveRefreshErrors. Clearing on push would let pushes mask a
  genuinely failing fetch; incrementing on push while only fetch cleared
  would be a one-way ratchet that could trip a reconnect on a healthy
  connection during an invite handshake.
- The scheduler is no longer Reset() on push. That existed to avoid a
  redundant read immediately behind the push; with the push tick no
  longer reading there is nothing redundant to avoid, and suppressing the
  safety poll because push fired is backwards - the poll is what catches
  whatever push misses, so a steady stream of pushes must not starve it.

Safe by construction: the safety poll still fetches on its own cadence,
so if the SDK ever fails to keep the local roster current, the worst case
degrades to poll latency rather than a stale list. Note the docs do NOT
explicitly promise Players stays current - the three lines of evidence
above are strong but indirect, which is exactly why the poll stays.

Unblocks relaxing the safety poll from 1.5s to 10s once push is confirmed
working in the editor (prefab-only change).

Ref: Docs/PresenceSystem/LIVENESS_COST_ANALYSIS.md § 5a, TODOS.md TODO-P9.
```

-            // happens". Reset the scheduler so the safety poll re-arms from now

### `c3dbf6820` — fix(party): a Relay 500 on boot no longer bricks the loading splash

_Claude, 2026-08-02 00:14:57 +0000_

```text
Reported intermittently on MPPM startup:

  [Multiplayer]: Internal Server Error: allocation call failure
  SessionException: Failed to create allocation
    ... PartySessionService.CreateAsync
    ... HostConnectionService.EnsurePartySessionAsync
    ... HostConnectionService.EnsureInitializedAsync
    ... HostConnectionService.HandleSignedInEvent   <- async void, UNHANDLED

Game then sits on the loading splash forever.

The 500 itself is upstream and transient. The brick is ours, in four
steps:

1. IsTransientSessionException did not match it. It matched five message
   patterns against the OUTER SessionException only - none of which is
   "Failed to create allocation" - and never walked InnerException, where
   the actual "Internal Server Error: allocation call failure" lives. So
   the existing 5-retry transient branch in CreateAsync never engaged.
2. Nothing caught it afterwards. EnsurePartySessionAsync and
   EnsureInitializedAsync both have `finally` but NO `catch`, so it
   propagated into the async void HandleSignedInEvent.
3. RaiseHostConnectionEstablished therefore never fired - and the auth
   scene waits on exactly that signal before loading Menu_Main. Permanent
   splash.
4. It could not self-recover either: the state machine was left in
   HostingParty, so IsInPresenceLobby was true and a re-fired sign-in hit
   EnsureInitializedAsync's early-return guard and did nothing.

Fixes:

- IsTransientSessionException now walks the InnerException chain and
  treats ANY UGS 5xx (RequestFailedException.ErrorCode 500-599) as
  transient - the general rule its five message patterns were specific
  instances of - plus explicit matches for the allocation-failure
  wording. CreateAsync's transient branch already retries 5x with
  exponential backoff, so the common case now self-heals.
- EnsurePartySessionAsync catches, logs with NetDiag, and raises
  OnHostConnectionLost. BootStatusBroadcaster listens for that and swaps
  the splash to the Retry surface, whose button routes back via
  bootStatusRetryRequestedEvent -> HandleBootStatusRetryRequested ->
  EnsurePartySessionAsync. The state machine is deliberately LEFT in
  HostingParty: IsHostingParty is false without a session, so the retry
  re-enters cleanly and skips a redundant transition. Rolling back to
  Disconnected would instead force a full re-init.
- EnsureInitializedAsync gains a matching backstop for everything before
  the session step (profile wait, identity sync, lobby join).

The rule: nothing on the boot path may throw into an async void. An
unhandled boot exception is indistinguishable from a hang.

Also corrects the XML doc on EnsureInitializedAsync, which still
described the retired LAZY / on-first-invite session model eight lines
above the code doing eager creation. That is the highest-risk doc drift
in the file - PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md flags it, I said I would fix it in
Commit 5, and I did not.

Known limitation: worst-case retry budget is ~31s, during which the
splash still reads "Loading" with no sign a retry is running. Surfacing
"retrying…" is a follow-up.

Logged as B13 in Docs/PresenceSystem/BUGS.md.
```

 3 files changed, 165 insertions(+), 17 deletions(-)
```

### `c49c8c91d` — fix(presence): repaint the roster row when presenceState changes

_Claude, 2026-08-02 00:43:12 +0000_

```text
Two instances, both ready in the lava lamp, each showing the other as
CONNECTING… - a regression I introduced in 641ec251.

RefreshOnlinePlayersDiff decides whether to replace a stored roster entry
with an inline five-field comparison. 641ec251 added PresenceState to
PartyPlayerData AND to ReadOnlinePlayerData, but not to that comparison.
So:

  1. A peer's row is created while they are still Announced (2) ->
     renders CONNECTING…
  2. They reach Present and publish presenceState = 3.
  3. The next diff reads the new value, compares the five fields it knows
     about, finds them identical, reports changed == false, and does NOT
     replace the entry.
  4. The stored row keeps PresenceState = 2 for the rest of the session.

Perfectly symmetric when two instances start together, since both rows
are created while both are Announced. It also explains why the earlier
4-instance run looked asymmetric and made B11 look like the whole story:
Ys1 started first, so its row on each peer happened to be created AFTER
it reached Present and captured 3 at insert time. B11 (the lost one-shot)
was real and its fix stands, but this was the larger half.

Fix: the comparison moves onto the struct as
PartyPlayerData.HasSameDisplayDataAs, living next to the fields it
compares, and the diff calls that. Adding a render-relevant field can no
longer silently skip the differ.

The general lesson, recorded in the struct: a type whose Equals is
deliberately identity-only (here, PlayerId, so ScriptableList dedup keeps
working while state fields change) needs an explicit content comparison
beside its fields. Left inlined at the call site it is guaranteed to go
stale the first time a field is added, and the failure is silent -
"no change detected" is indistinguishable from "nothing changed".

PartyMemberService.SyncFromSession is unaffected: it compares
DisplayName/AvatarId only, correct for its data source, and builds rows
through the 3-arg constructor which defaults presenceState to Present.

Logged as B14.
```

                     {

@@ -96,5 +102,32 @@ namespace CosmicShore.ScriptableObjects

+        /// <summary>
+        /// True when every field a roster row RENDERS from is identical - i.e.
+        /// replacing the stored entry with <paramref name="other"/> would change
+        /// nothing on screen.
+        ///
+        /// <para>
+        /// <b>This is the single list of render-relevant fields. Add new ones
+        /// here.</b> The diff in <c>HostConnectionService.RefreshOnlinePlayersDiff</c>
+        /// used to inline this comparison, and when <see cref="PresenceState"/>
+        /// was added to this struct and to the property reader, the inline
+        /// comparison was not updated. The consequence was invisible and total: a
+        /// peer's row was created while they were still `Announced`, they
+        /// published `Present` a moment later, the diff read the new value,
+        /// compared the five fields it knew about, concluded "unchanged", and
+        /// never replaced the entry - so every peer rendered them as
+        /// "CONNECTING…" permanently. Keeping the comparison next to the fields
+        /// makes that omission structurally impossible to repeat.
+        /// </para>
+        /// </summary>
+        public bool HasSameDisplayDataAs(PartyPlayerData other) =>
+            displayName      == other.displayName      &&
+            avatarId         == other.avatarId         &&
+            partyMemberCount == other.partyMemberCount &&

+            MatchName        == other.MatchName        &&
+            presenceState    == other.presenceState;
     }

### `dd1df6dec` — fix(diagnostics): stackless benign-skip line; record the measured fault rate

_Claude, 2026-08-02 02:30:54 +0000_

```text
The benign-skip diagnostic added in Commit 1 was routed through
CSDebug.Log -> Debug.Log, and Unity attaches a full managed stack to
every Debug.Log. Because this line fires on a timer from deep inside an
async chain, each occurrence printed ~100 console lines of UGS/UniTask
plumbing - identical every time - which buries the counters that are the
actual signal, and pays for a stack walk it never uses.

Adds CSDebug.LogNoStack (Debug.LogFormat with LogOption.NoStacktrace) and
uses it there. Editor/development only, like the rest of CSDebug.

Also records the first real measurement in BUGS.md - the data
PresenceSystem/REFACTOR.md's LobbyMembershipMonitor extraction and
TODO-P5 have been explicitly blocked on:

  presence read:      13 voided / ~64 ticks  = ~20%
  party-session read: 22 voided / ~51 ticks  = ~43%

over ~96s, thrown from WrappedLobbyService.GetLobbyAsync - the HTTP GET
succeeded and the SDK failed deserialising it against its stale local
cache. That is the B6 read-path surface, live and firing hard.

Three consequences worth acting on, recorded with it:

- Do NOT relax the safety poll 1.5s -> 10s yet. That was staged as a
  prefab-only change once push was confirmed, but at a ~20% void rate a
  10s nominal poll is a ~12.5s effective backstop, and the party-session
  path is worse.
- TODO-P2 (coalesce startup property writes) is now motivated by data
  rather than speculation: fewer writes means fewer deltas means fewer
  stale-index opportunities.
- LobbyMembershipMonitor must treat SdkStaleIndex as explicitly NOT
  membership loss - at this rate an error-count watchdog would escalate
  constantly.
```

### `63234841d` — docs(presence): record the MPPM retest — B11/B13/B14 verified, B12 mistested

_Claude, 2026-08-04 20:41:41 +0000_

```text
Owner ran the branch in MPPM. Three fixes confirmed, one apparent failure
that turned out to be a bad test of mine, and a second fault-rate sample.

VERIFIED
- B11 (presence stuck at Announced) and B14 (presenceState never repainted
  the row) are both closed. They had to be fixed together: B11 dropped the
  signal, B14 dropped the repaint that would have shown it arriving.
- B13 (Relay 500 bricks the boot splash) passed a 4-instance run. Kept at
  "wider run pending" — the fault is upstream and intermittent, so one clean
  run is weak evidence that the retry path works.

B12 — the retest was invalid, and the bad test was mine
Deactivating an MPPM virtual player TERMINATES the clone process. No
wantsToQuit, no playModeStateChanged, no code runs at all. It is a hard
kill, which PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md § 6 case (c) already says cannot beat the
UGS reap. The observed 30-50 s is therefore the correct result, not a
regression.

The error was in the verification guide's departure table, which listed
"MPPM: toggle the virtual player off / stop play mode" as ONE row at <1 s.
Those are two different mechanisms and only the second runs any code — and
the second stops every virtual player simultaneously, so it leaves no
observer either.

Consequence worth stating plainly: MPPM structurally cannot test a graceful
departure. The named-id eviction path (TryConsumeDepartedPlayerIds) added
for B12 has never executed in any test — every departure observed so far
went through the reap. B12 stays 🟡, and the sub-second claim for a graceful
exit is design intent, not a measured result. Verifying it needs a
standalone build quit next to a running editor, or the editor-only hook now
tracked as TODO-P10.

Also: the measured reap is 30-50 s, not the ~30 s estimated in the code.

MEASURED (run 2): presence=16, partySession=39 over 206.5 s
Both rates fell by roughly a third (~20%→~12% presence, ~43%→~32%
party-session) after 40226752 stopped push ticks fetching. The more useful
signal is the SHAPE: run 2 covers 2.2x the wall time but carries only 1.2x
the presence skips. A per-tick fault rate that decays with session age fires
mostly in the opening seconds — which is exactly the multi-client property
write burst B1 describes.

That promotes TODO-P2 (coalesce startup property writes) from speculative to
the highest-value code item on the list, with a numeric pass criterion it
did not have before. It also keeps the safety-poll relaxation blocked: at
~12% a 10 s nominal poll is a ~11.4 s effective backstop.

TODO-P5 is answered — the class mix is overwhelmingly Transient/SdkStaleIndex
with no SessionGone and no Offline in either run. LobbyMembershipMonitor is
unblocked and must treat SdkStaleIndex as explicitly NOT membership loss;
the StaleReference state is required, RemovedFromLobby has no evidence yet.

Files
- BUGS.md — statuses; run-2 measurement; B12 retest analysis.
- TESTS.md — P5 rewritten around the three departure classes; the old single
  "within 5 s" criterion made a correct build look broken.
- PRESENCE_SYNC_VERIFICATION.md — progress table; corrected Step 2d; Step 1c
  answered; follow-up list reconciled.
- PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN.md § 6 — new case (e) recording that case (a) has no
  test harness.
- TODOS.md — priority table; TODO-P10 added; P2 promoted; P5 answered.
- UNITY_VERIFICATION_CHECKLIST.md — consolidated branch status.
```

### `3e8885c2d` — fix(party): render your own party's size from the local roster, not a peer's claim

_Claude, 2026-08-05 19:54:28 +0000_

```text
Three players in ONE party saw three different sizes at the same instant
(A: B=2/4, C=1/4 · B: A=3/4, C=3/4 · C: wrong like A).

Root cause: FriendsListPanel.ResolveRemoteStatus assigned the member count
once, up front, from the REMOTE peer's self-published `partyCount` presence
property - and used it for every status including InYourParty. So the bucket
("is this person in my party?") came from local truth via IsInSameParty, while
the number came from the peer's stale self-report.

With N members that is N independently-published scalars and N*(N-1)
independently-lossy read edges, and nothing that reconciles them. This is a
wrong-source-of-truth bug, not a latency bug: no poll cadence can make N
scalars agree, which is why previous cadence/push/jitter work never fixed it.
It violated two things already written down - the locked "session is
authoritative over presence - the lobby is a hint, never the source of truth"
invariant, and PartySystem/ARCHITECTURE.md exit criterion 3 ("host's view of
party membership matches every client's within one refresh tick").

FriendsListPanel:386 was the only display consumer of the advertised count in
the codebase; ArcadeGameConfigureModal, QuickPlayButton, ScreenSwitcher and
FriendsInitializer all already read the local roster. A lone outlier.

The fix decides the bucket FIRST, then sources the numbers from the matching
tier:

  "how big is MY party?"    -> IPartyRoster       (local, authoritative, 0 latency)
  "how big is THEIR party?" -> Advertised* fields (presence lobby, a hint)

- IPartyRoster: read-only local-roster query surface (MemberCount, MaxSlots,
  HasOpenSlots, Contains, IsHost), implemented by HostConnectionDataSO. No new
  writer, no new DI registration, no inspector wiring - every party-aware panel
  already holds the SO. The active session id deliberately stays on
  IPartyStateQuery.ActivePartySessionId; one owner per fact.
- PartyPlayerData.PartyMemberCount/PartyMaxSlots -> AdvertisedPartyMemberCount/
  AdvertisedPartyMaxSlots. Property renames only; the serialized backing fields
  are untouched, so no asset re-serialization. The name is the enforcement - it
  can no longer be mistaken for truth at a call site.
- FriendsListPanel.IsInSameParty now delegates to IPartyRoster.Contains rather
  than walking PartyMembers itself, and the dead IsInSameParty re-check in the
  LobbyFull branch is dropped (the InYourParty branch already returned).
- Roster collapses Unity's fake-null once at the boundary: an interface-typed
  reference would compare with plain reference equality and report a destroyed
  SO as alive.

Documents the live trap for the next person: PartyMembers entries are built by
PartyMemberService.ReadMemberData through the identity-only ctor, so each one
reports AdvertisedPartyMemberCount == 0. Pointing a label at a member's own
advertised field would render 0/4. MemberCount counts the list instead.

Fixes the reported divergence and makes the local repaint instant (the count
no longer depends on any network read). Cross-machine propagation latency -
the 15-20s "1/4 -> 2/4" with its voided party-session reads - is untouched
here and is the subject of the follow-up commits.

Tests: new PartyRosterTests (17 cases) pin that MemberCount counts the list
rather than reading a field off its items, includes the local player, and that
IsHost tracks IsPartyHost and not IsPresenceLobbyHost.

Verification: conditional-compilation gate green (1549 files). Full A/B/C MPPM
repro with UNIQUE VP TAGS still required - untagged clones share the
mppm-clone auth profile and reproduce this exact symptom for an unrelated
reason.
```

### `f6aecb6a5` — feat(party): coalesced OnPartyRosterChanged channel, raised once per settled mutation

_Claude, 2026-08-06 21:45:36 +0000_

```text
Three consumers - FriendsListPanel, ArcadeLobbyList, FriendsInitializer - each
subscribed to OnPartyMemberJoined, OnPartyMemberLeft AND OnPartyMemberKicked and
answered all three with the same full repopulate. A sync moving three members
therefore rebuilt every panel three times, and each rebuild ran against a roster
that was still mid-mutation.

Adds HostConnectionDataSO.OnPartyRosterChanged (ScriptableEventNoParam), raised
by SoapPartyEventBus.RaisePartyRosterChanged - which stays the sole permitted
raiser of events on this container. Two properties make it worth having, and
both are pinned by tests:

  coalescing   N member moves cost exactly 1 raise
  change gate  a no-op costs 0 raises

The change gate is load-bearing. Without it the signal would fire on every
steady-state poll tick, which is precisely the per-tick churn the channel exists
to remove.

Raised from every one of the roster's mutation sites, which had to be enumerated
because two of them bypass PartyMemberService entirely:

  PartyMemberService.SeedLocalPlayer / SyncFromSession / ClearSilent / ClearWithEvents
  HostConnectionDataSO.RemovePartyMember          (the kick path)
  HostConnectionService.ScanPresenceForJoinedPartyMembers  (host-only presence scan)

Always after the per-member events and after the list has settled, so a listener
can never observe a half-applied roster and _outgoingInvitePlayerIds is already
current by the time the repaint runs.

Deliberately NOT raised from ResetRuntimeData: it runs during scene transitions
before the old scene's objects are destroyed, the lists' own OnCleared already
drives the panels, and a second inline raise into objects mid-teardown buys
nothing.

Consumer changes:
- FriendsListPanel splits its handler. The pending-invite-tint clear is the only
  part that needs to know WHO moved, so it stays on the per-member events; the
  repaint moves to the coalesced channel.
- ArcadeLobbyList drops all three subscriptions - its handler discarded the
  PartyPlayerData payload outright. The PartyMembers list events stay as the
  pre-existing backstop; they fire mid-mutation, this fires once the roster is
  whole.
- FriendsInitializer replaces a joined-handler plus an only-when-empty
  left-handler with one roster handler. That pair had a hole: a 3->2 shrink hit
  neither branch, so the advertised FriendPresenceActivity.PartyMemberCount
  stayed pinned at 3 for the rest of the session. Gated on the remote count
  actually changing, so a seed or clear that leaves the count where it was costs
  no UGS presence write and cannot overwrite an in-game activity if
  SetPresenceInGame is ever wired up (it has no production callers today).

Asset output IS the deliverable here, per Docs/TOOLING.md - the new
Event_PartyRosterChanged.asset and its wiring into HostConnectionData.asset are
committed alongside the code. Per project policy the new SOAP field carries no
if-null guard at the raise site inside the bus; the subscribe sites keep the
same defensive shape as every other event on this container.

Tests: PartyRosterEventTests (13 cases) - coalescing, the settled-roster
guarantee, the change gate on every path, and the kick path that bypasses
PartyMemberService.

Verification: conditional-compilation gate green (1550 files). 3-VP MPPM smoke
(S1-S4, S9) still required.
```

### `111676ba2` — fix(party): republish presence the moment the roster moves, not on the next good poll

_Claude, 2026-08-06 21:46:47 +0000_

```text
Our published `partyCount` is a function of the local roster, but nothing
republished it when that roster changed. PublishPresenceImmediateAsync fired only
on Menu_Main load and on InMatch/Present presence transitions, so a new party size
reached the wire only on the next SUCCESSFUL poll tick - and the presence read
gating that tick is voided ~12% of the time by the SDK stale-index defect
(Docs/PresenceSystem/BUGS.md, MEASURED run 2). Peers kept rendering a stale party
size for seconds after the party had actually changed.

HostConnectionService now subscribes OnPartyRosterChanged and publishes on it.

The handler sets a flag instead of publishing inline, which is load-bearing rather
than stylistic: the roster raise fires from inside RefreshAsync and
ScanPresenceForJoinedPartyMembers, both already holding _lobbyMutex, and
PublishPresenceImmediateAsync waits on that same mutex - an inline call would
deadlock the refresh cycle against itself. Update() drains the flag, and only
reaches the drain after its own gates have established the mutex is free.

The flag debounces too: several roster changes in one frame collapse to one
publish, and PublishPartyStateIfChangedAsync's existing change-gate makes a
publish whose values did not actually move free.

Unsubscribed in OnDestroy. HostConnectionDataSO is a project asset that outlives
every scene and every instance of this service, so a leaked handler would keep
firing on a destroyed component for the rest of the session - the same class of
leak Docs/ScoringSystem/BUGS.md B15 documents for RoundStats.

Verification: conditional-compilation gate green (1550 files). 3-VP MPPM smoke
(S1-S4, S9) still required.
```

### `791c6d045` — fix(party): the presence publish no longer rides on the presence read succeeding

_Claude, 2026-08-06 21:49:45 +0000_

```text
RefreshAsync was one linear pipeline inside ONE try: lobby read -> converge ->
online diff -> invite scan -> acceptance scan -> joined-member scan -> party
member sync -> publish. The lobby read is the FIRST await, so a fault there
unwound past everything below it - including our own partyCount write.

That read is voided ~12% of poll ticks by the UGS stale-index defect
(Docs/PresenceSystem/BUGS.md, MEASURED run 2), which made "my party size reaches
my peers" depend on a coin flip we do not control. Reading the lobby and
publishing into it are independent operations; they now fail independently.

The read section gets its own try, and only the two BENIGN classifications are
absorbed there. Everything else - rate limit, definite, transient - still
propagates to the outer catch with its recovery matrix completely untouched:
those mean the lobby itself is in trouble and publishing into it is pointless.

The benign branches move from an if/else chain to exception filters, which
required making the documented branch ordering explicit rather than positional:

    catch (Exception e) when (!IsRateLimitException(e) && IsBenignSdkStaleIndexError(e))

IsBenignSdkStaleIndexError matches ANY SessionException whose Error is Unknown,
and a wrapped 429 can present in exactly that shape. Without the guard a filter
would absorb a throttle without arming the backoff - the precise bug the original
"[rate-limit] MUST be tested before the benign branches" comment exists to
prevent. Excluded, it falls through to the outer rate-limit branch as before.

The consecutive-error counter clear moves inside the inner try, so a voided read
no longer counts as evidence of a healthy connection either.

Also corrects the diagnostic the owner actually reads. It claimed
"roster diff / invite scan / member sync / publish all skipped this tick" - now
false for the presence path, and it was always false for the party-session path
(RefreshPartyMembersAsync catches internally, so the publish already ran). Now:
"READS VOIDED this tick (... skipped; the presence publish still ran)".

Deviation from the plan, stated plainly: this commit was scoped as "extract
PresencePublisher, decouple from the read". The decoupling is the fix and it is
done; the extraction is not. The published-value trackers are entangled with
_presence.CurrentState, ResolveCurrentMatchName, _propertyWriter and
_lobbyService, so pulling them into a class means threading four dependencies or
a delegate through - more surface area than the change buys today, in the most
fragile area of the codebase. Left as a follow-up rather than done badly.

Verification: conditional-compilation gate green (1550 files). 3-VP MPPM smoke
(S1-S4, S9) plus Stress-1/2/3 required - this touches the refresh error matrix.
```

### `090f61a6b` — feat(party): push channel on the party session - a join no longer waits for a poll read

_Claude, 2026-08-06 21:52:26 +0000_

```text
The presence lobby has had a push channel since PRESENCE_SYNC_PLAN commit 4: seven
ISession events, flag-only handlers, drained on the main thread. The party session
had exactly ONE subscription - PlayerLeaving - and no roster-dirty flag at all.

So a party JOIN was discoverable only by RefreshPartyMembersAsync on a poll tick,
and that read is voided ~32% of the time by the SDK stale-index defect
(Docs/PresenceSystem/BUGS.md, MEASURED run 2). Two voided ticks plus the 4s
creation grace period is the reported "IN YOUR PARTY 1/4 -> 2/4 took 15-20 seconds
with 2 VOIDED logs".

PartySessionService gains the same push channel: PlayerJoined, PlayerHasLeft,
PlayerPropertiesChanged and Changed set an Interlocked int; PlayerLeaving keeps
its typed relay (the host's invite-clear and reconcile need the departing id, not
just "something moved") and now marks the flag too. Wired at both ActiveSession
assignments, unwired in ClearSession - the single point that nulls the reference.
Handlers do nothing but set the flag: per Docs/THREADING.md the SDK gives no
thread guarantee and a SOAP Raise() invokes listeners inline, so touching Unity
state there would throw EnsureRunningOnMainThread from FriendsListPanel.

The decisive property is what the push path does NOT do:

  SyncPartyMembersFromCacheAsync issues no UGS call at all.

UGS patches ISession.Players before it raises, so the roster is already correct
locally when we drain. A path with no read cannot be voided by a read fault. The
poll read becomes the backstop rather than the mechanism - the same bargain
TODO-P9 already banked on the presence side.

RefreshPartyMembersAsync now ends by calling that same method, so push and poll
share one diff implementation and cannot drift apart.

Two placement decisions worth stating, both deliberate:

- The drain sits ABOVE the presence-lobby gates in Update(). The two sessions are
  independent layers (locked two-level design), so a discovery-layer problem must
  not freeze the party roster; and since the drain makes no UGS call, gating it on
  the lobby mutex would stall the roster during an in-flight property write -
  exactly when party state is changing, the same trap that once made the scheduler
  measure eligible time instead of wall time.
- The push path is NOT gated on SESSION_CREATION_GRACE_PERIOD_SECONDS. That gate
  protects a freshly-provisioned session's READ from 404ing into recovery that
  kicks a joining client. There is no read here to fail, and suppressing the push
  during precisely the window when someone is joining would forfeit the benefit.

RemovedFromSession and Deleted are deliberately NOT subscribed. Party-session loss
already routes through MultiplayerSetup.OnClientDisconnect ->
PartyInviteController.HandleHostLossAsync; a second recovery trigger on a
different signal would race it, and this commit is about the join-discovery gap.

Verification: conditional-compilation gate green (1550 files). Needs the full
3-VP MPPM smoke (S1-S4, S9) plus Stress-1/2/3, and the A/B/C repro timing: the
1/4 -> 2/4 transition should now land within a frame of the join rather than
15-20s, with the benign-skip counters still climbing (the SDK defect is
untouched) but no longer costing correctness.
```

+        /// <summary>
+        /// Diffs <c>HostConnectionDataSO.PartyMembers</c> against the SDK's
+        /// IN-MEMORY session roster. Issues no UGS call whatsoever.
+        ///
+        /// <para>
+        /// <b>This is the push path, and its cost profile is the point.</b> UGS
+        /// patches <c>ISession.Players</c> before it raises
+        /// <c>PlayerJoined</c> / <c>PlayerHasLeft</c> / <c>Changed</c>, so by the
+        /// time we drain the flag the roster is already correct locally. A path
+        /// with no read cannot be voided by a read fault - and the party-session
+        /// read is voided ~32% of poll ticks by the SDK stale-index defect
+        /// (<c>Docs/PresenceSystem/BUGS.md</c>, MEASURED run 2). Discovering a
+        /// join used to depend entirely on that read; now the read is only the
+        /// backstop.
+        /// </para>
+        ///
+        /// <para>
+        /// Also called by <see cref="RefreshPartyMembersAsync"/> as the tail of
+        /// the poll path, after its read has succeeded - one diff implementation
+        /// for both, so push and poll can never drift apart.
+        /// </para>
+        /// </summary>
+        private async UniTask SyncPartyMembersFromCacheAsync()
+        {
+            var session = _partySessionService.ActiveSession;
+            if (session == null) return;
+            if (connectionData.PartyMembers == null) return;
+

             // reconcile tick rather than risk adding our own session player as a phantom.
             string localId = ResolveLocalPlayerId();
             if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(localId))
                 return;

### `4129b9321` — refactor(party): one owner for the wire format, and stop writing six dead keys

_Claude, 2026-08-06 21:55:48 +0000_

```text
Twelve property-key literals were declared independently in five files -
HostConnectionService, PresenceLobbyService, PartySessionService,
PartyMemberService, AcceptanceSignalService - each redeclaring the subset it
happened to need. The wire format had five partial definitions and nothing that
could be read to learn it.

That cost is not hypothetical. `presenceState` existed in exactly ONE of those
files, which is why a converge migration rebuilt the player record from a builder
that had never heard of the key, silently dropped it, and left peers rendering
"CONNECTING…" forever (Docs/PresenceSystem/BUGS.md B11/B14). A key only one
writer knows about is a key the other writers will drop.

Adds PartyLobbyKeys as the single owner. Existing declarations become forwarding
constants (`private const string DISPLAY_NAME_KEY = PartyLobbyKeys.DisplayName;`)
rather than being deleted, for two reasons: ~100 call sites stay untouched, so
this carries no behavioural risk; and PartyInviteSystemTests reflects on
HostConnectionService's INVITE_PAYLOADS_KEY / DISPLAY_NAME_KEY / AVATAR_ID_KEY by
name, which the ARCHITECTURE doc freezes. Forwarding consts are still
compile-time constants, so reflection returns the same values.

Second half: PartySessionService.BuildLocalPlayerProperties wrote EIGHT keys onto
the Relay session; the session's player properties have exactly one reader in the
codebase - PartyMemberService.ReadMemberData, which takes displayName and
avatarId. The other six (partyCount, partyMax, matchName, joined_party,
invite_payloads, accepted_invite) are the presence lobby's vocabulary; every
consumer of them reads from _lobbyService.ActiveLobby.Players, never from the
session. Four were always string.Empty. Verified by tracing every
Properties.TryGetValue call site to the collection it iterates.

Removing them is not tidiness. Property writes are the measured driver of the
B1/B6 stale-index defect - the fault rate concentrates in the startup write burst,
which is why "coalesce startup property writes" (TODO-P2) is the highest-value
item on the presence list. Session create and join each carried six dead keys;
now they carry none. This is a partial down-payment on TODO-P2, not a substitute
for it.

Tests: PartyLobbyKeysTests pins every key's VALUE as a frozen wire contract -
renaming a constant is free, changing its value breaks cross-version parties
silently (the reader finds no key and falls back to a default that looks like
ordinary staleness). A second test fails if a key is added to the class without
being added to the frozen table, and three more assert the forwarding constants
have not drifted from the owner.

Verification: conditional-compilation gate green (1552 files). No raw key
literals remain anywhere under Controller/Party except in PartyLobbyKeys itself
and in doc comments. 3-VP MPPM smoke (S1-S4, S9) still required.
```

### `9ee67e5f2` — fix(party): fail loud when OnPartyRosterChanged is unwired

_Claude, 2026-08-10 19:48:48 +0000_

```text
OnPartyRosterChanged is now the ONLY repaint signal FriendsListPanel and
ArcadeLobbyList listen to for party changes - the per-member subscriptions they
used to repaint from were removed when the coalesced channel replaced them. A
SOAP raise on a null reference is a silent no-op, so an unwired asset does not
degrade the party UI, it freezes it, with nothing in the console to say why.

Logs Debug.LogError at Start with the field name, the asset name and the fix,
per the locked "every null guard logs Debug.LogError with field name and
suspected cause" rule in Docs/PartySystem/ARCHITECTURE.md.

The asset IS wired on HostConnectionData.asset in 4129b932; this guards the case
where someone creates a second HostConnectionData, or a merge drops the field.
```

### `4aece9257` — fix(party): defer the roster-changed raise to the main thread

_Claude, 2026-08-10 22:29:41 +0000_

```text
Regression I introduced in f6aecb6a. The user hit EnsureRunningOnMainThread errors
in a live build after this branch, in a system that had been working.

ROOT CAUSE. SOAP invokes listeners INLINE on the calling thread. OnPartyRosterChanged's
listeners touch UnityEngine.Object:

  FriendsInitializer.HandlePartyRosterChanged  ->  `hostConnectionData == null`
                                                   (op_Equality) then an async void
                                                   UGS call. Lives on a PERSISTENT
                                                   GameObject, so it is always attached.
  FriendsListPanel.HandlePartyRosterChanged    ->  PopulateOnlineSection -> Instantiate/Destroy
  ArcadeLobbyList.HandlePartyRosterChanged     ->  PopulateSlots

Its request sites are not all main-thread. PartyMemberService.SeedLocalPlayer is called
by HostConnectionService.ApplyPostLobbyJoinState at four sites, each immediately after
`await _lobbyService.JoinOrCreateAsync(...)`. That method's fallback path is
`await CreateAsync(...)` then `await UniTask.Delay(...)` then ConvergeToCanonicalAsync,
which can return without awaiting anything at all. Per Docs/THREADING.md, awaiting a
UniTask does NOT restore the caller's thread and UniTask's own primitives do not
reliably marshal to main on this version - so the continuation can land on the
ThreadPool, and the raise took the UI with it.

Before this branch SeedLocalPlayer raised NO SOAP event; it only mutated the
PartyMembers ScriptableList, and FriendsListPanel subscribes solely to OnlinePlayers
list events. So that path could not reach any of this. The coalesced channel connected
it - including one always-live listener - which is why the errors appeared in a build
that had been fine.

FIX. RaisePartyRosterChanged becomes RequestPartyRosterChanged (Interlocked flag, safe
from any thread) plus FlushPartyRosterChanged (the actual raise), drained once per frame
at the very top of HostConnectionService.Update - before every gate including
IsOnMenuScene, so a roster change can never be stranded unraised, and before the
publish drain so the publish still lands in the same frame.

Deferring once at the bus fixes all six request sites together. The alternative -
making each listener thread-safe - is three fixes instead of one, and the next listener
anyone adds reintroduces the bug. This is also the exact Interlocked-flag-drained-from-
Update pattern PresenceLobbyService already uses for every UGS push; I should have
followed it when I added the channel.

Coalescing gets strictly better as a side effect: several roster changes in one frame
now collapse to one repaint instead of one repaint each. Cost is one frame of latency,
which nothing waits on - the count is local after C1.

HostConnectionDataSO.RemovePartyMember keeps its direct raise and is now documented
MAIN THREAD ONLY: its sole caller invokes it before its first await and is only ever
reached from a UI button handler.

Tests: the existing 13 cases are rewired to drain the bus (a RosterRaises accessor
stands in for the Update drain), and three new cases pin the deferral itself -
that a request does NOT raise inline, that an empty flush is free, and that multiple
requests before a flush collapse to one raise. Also fixes a latent bug in my own
test: SeedLocalPlayer_AlreadySeeded left a pending flag that the later flush would
have misattributed.

Verification: conditional-compilation gate green (1552 files); missing-using sweep
clean across all changed files. NOT yet run in the editor - single-instance play mode
in Menu_Main is the gate, watching for EnsureRunningOnMainThread and the
SceneTransitionManager main-thread canary.
```

 Assets/_Scripts/Controller/Party/Services/PartyMemberService.cs |  10 ++--

 Assets/_Scripts/Tests/EditMode/PartyRosterEventTests.cs         | 116 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++-----

 5 files changed, 201 insertions(+), 36 deletions(-)
```

### `5b36156e9` — refactor(party ui): retire the PARTY FULL status; IN PARTY 4/4 says it already

_Claude, 2026-08-11 22:33:38 +0000_

```text
"PARTY FULL" was a second way of stating what the count already showed, so it is
gone - a full remote party now renders through the normal InLobby branch as
"IN PARTY 4/4".

The catch, found before touching anything: Status.LobbyFull was NOT just a label.
OnlineInfoEntry gated invitability on `status == Online || status == InLobby`, and
LobbyFull's absence from that list is what made a full party non-invitable. Deleting
the status naively would have silently made full parties invitable - a behaviour
change wearing a cosmetic change's clothes.

So the rule moves rather than disappearing. OnlineInfoEntry.Populate already receives
partyMemberCount and partyMaxSlots, so it derives fullness itself:

    bool targetPartyFull = partyMaxSlots > 0 && partyMemberCount >= partyMaxSlots;
    _invitable = onInvite != null
                 && (status == Status.Online || status == Status.InLobby)
                 && !targetPartyFull;

Behaviour is identical in every case - full remote party non-invitable and dimmed,
3/4 party invitable, lone player invitable, own party members non-invitable via a
status that was never in the invitable list anyway. The label and the "can I invite
them" rule now read the SAME two numbers, so they cannot drift apart the way they
could when one lived in a status and the other in a label.

Per the owner's decision: full-party players stay blocked from being invited, and the
label inherits the blue IN PARTY colour rather than keeping a special grey.

Also removes the now-dead lobbyFullColor field and corrects five stale references to
the retired state (ArcadeLobbyList, FriendsListPanel, two Populate param docs,
PartyPlayerData's "legitimate readers" note).

Audited the other five statuses for the same redundancy; only this one was removable,
and two deserve to be recorded as deliberately kept:
  - Connecting is load-bearing. It marks a peer whose vessel has not spawned, i.e.
    whose own refresh loop is not running, so an invite to them is never scanned for.
    Cutting it re-opens "invite silently vanishes" (PresenceSystem BUGS B11/B14).
  - InMatch is genuinely live now. It was dead for a long time because matchName was
    only published from a path gated on the menu scene; ResolveCurrentMatchName keys
    off PresenceState.InMatch since that fix.

Documents four invite-system cleanup candidates in PartySystem/TODOS.md (TODO-10..13)
rather than acting on any of them - most notably that the PENDING sentinel appears
dead under eager Relay (no code path writes one; everything either patches or
excludes it), which is REFACTOR.md D1 with evidence attached. Each entry carries its
evidence, what breaks if the evidence is wrong, and an instrument-first method,
because that conclusion rests on a grep rather than a run and it sits inside the
accept handshake that only just became reliable.

Note: the OnlineFriendsInfo prefab still carries a serialized lobbyFullColor value.
Unity ignores properties that no longer resolve to a field and drops them on the next
save of that prefab - left alone rather than hand-editing prefab YAML.

Verification: conditional-compilation gate green (1552 files); using sweep clean; no
test references LobbyFull, so no test updates were needed. UI only - no SO assets, no
networking, no async surface. Needs the 4-VP repro of a party you are NOT in reaching
capacity, which is the case this actually changes.
```

### `e282be1c4` — docs(party): close out the roster-truth branch - B15/B16 verified, gates and next steps

_Claude, 2026-08-12 22:59:51 +0000_

```text
Session wrap-up. The party system is owner-verified at 4 VPs (invite, accept,
party formation, cross-panel agreement), so this records what shipped, what was
learned, and what the next branch should pick up.

PresenceSystem/BUGS.md
- B15 marked VERIFIED FIXED 2026-08-06.
- B16 added in full: the self-inflicted threading regression. A new SOAP event
  was raised from PartyMemberService.SeedLocalPlayer, which runs after an await
  of JoinOrCreateAsync with no main-thread guarantee, and one of its listeners
  (FriendsInitializer, on a persistent GameObject) does a UnityEngine.Object null
  check. Documents the mechanism, why it was NEW (SeedLocalPlayer previously
  raised nothing and FriendsListPanel subscribes only to OnlinePlayers), the
  deferral fix, and the rule it establishes: a SOAP event whose listeners touch
  Unity state may only be raised from a guaranteed main-thread context, otherwise
  defer to an Update() drain. Also records the process failure honestly - seven
  commits pushed with no runtime verification between them, sequenced by value
  instead of blast radius.

PartySystem/TESTS.md
- New "Verification ORDER - cheapest gate first" table at the top. Step 2 is
  single-editor play mode watching for EnsureRunningOnMainThread and the
  SceneTransitionManager canary; it costs a minute, needs no virtual players, and
  would have caught B16 that a full MPPM plan missed. Mandatory on any commit that
  adds or moves a SOAP raise.
- S10: party-size agreement across every panel (the B15 gate), including the
  counter-intuitive pass criterion that the benign-skip counters SHOULD still
  climb - correct counts despite them is the proof the read/publish split works.
- S11: a full party you are NOT in stays non-invitable. Notes explicitly that the
  ordinary 4-in-one-party case does not exercise this, since those rows render
  through InYourParty.

PartySystem/REFACTOR.md
- Full commit table for the branch including the R1 threading fix and the status
  cleanup, marked verified.
- "Read this before the next branch here" - B16's rule plus the sequencing lesson.

PartySystem/TODOS.md
- Prioritised next-steps table (value per risk), with TODO-P2 first and explicit
  notes that each item is its own branch. Carries forward the two standing
  blocks: safety-poll relaxation (blocked on TODO-P2) and the heartbeat (closed).
```

+**step 2 alone would have caught in under a minute** — no virtual players, no
+party, no invite.

+| # | Gate | Cost | Catches |
+|---|---|---|---|

+| 3 | 3-VP MPPM smoke: S1–S4, S9 | ~10 min | the party lifecycle |
+| 4 | Stress-1/2/3 (refactor commits only) | ~15 min | races, re-entrancy |

+**Step 2 is not optional on any commit that adds or moves a SOAP raise.** It is
+the only gate in this list that is cheap enough to run on every single commit,
+and it is the one that failed to exist when it was needed.

     real but not urgent.

classifier is the subtle part of that commit.

and points at the list.

Degrades rather than breaks — worst case a benign SDK fault is treated as definite and recreates a solo session mid-join. **This is the first commit to revert** if something odd surfaces later: it is self-contained, and its benefit (the publish surviving a voided read) is real but not urgent. |
+
+**How to provoke #4 if you want it covered.** Rate-limit is reachable by spamming
+invite/cancel until UGS 429s — you should see

+skip. The precedence between those two is the subtle part of that commit

+practically reachable without fault injection; treat them as reviewed-by-reading
+until there is a reason to do more.

do-not-reuse, along with CellularDuel (8), WildlifeBlitz (26),
MultiplayerFreestyle (28), Multiplayer2v2CoOpVsAI (30) and
MultiplayerWildlifeBlitzGame (32). None is reinstated here.

The test asserted that an unmapped mode falls back to Score, using a
legacy mode as the stand-in. That premise no longer exists, so the case
is carried by the live modes that genuinely reach the fallback arm -
Benchmark (whose GameModes.cs comment names the "comeback default" as a
consumer it exists to let resolve honestly instead of borrowing a
retired id), Tournament (a meta whose chained minigames each set their
own mode) and Random. Renamed to
DefaultSourceFor_UnmappedModesFallBackToScore, since nothing
time-scored remains to name.

Also refreshed the matching comment on DefaultSourceFor's default arm,
which justified the fallback by naming all four deleted modes. Both
sites now record that those IDs must not come back.

Verified: Roslyn parse of both files clean; a sweep over every
GameModes.X reference in Assets (excluding third-party) resolves
against the live enum; check_conditional_compilation.py OK.
```
